GRIN2D ↔ GPR6 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.67 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows that the modeled GRIN2D–GPR6 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That is topologically impossible across an intact membrane, so the large interface (3225 Å², 639 contacts, interface pLDDT 63.7) is most likely an artifact of folding two transmembrane proteins as free sequences. This undercuts my earlier LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX call. That call treated the missing GRIN1 subunit as the main caveat and ignored the topology problem. Adding GRIN1 would not fix a contact that is impossible in the membrane. The rest of the evidence is unchanged and gives no independent support: STRING and CellPhoneDB have no entry for the pair, PubMed co-occurrence is 0, and the supplied ipTM of 0.67 is only moderate. Brain co-expression of the two proteins is the only argument left, and it does not outweigh a fold that violates membrane topology. Glycosylation is a minor point here, because the interface itself is topologically invalid.
PTM / glycosylation
GRIN2D is a glycoprotein with 6 glycosylation sites. GPR6 is a glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites and is palmitoylated. This is secondary to the topology violation, and no per-residue interface data were available.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 hits for GRIN2D together with GPR6.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction, and GPR6 is not in its gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. The known_interaction_strength check is "none", so the pair is novel but has no literature support.
Other subunits
GRIN1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Only if there is a specific biological reason to suspect a GRIN2D–GPR6 interaction: re-fold with GRIN1 and a second GluN2 subunit, then check that any interface lies within a single topological compartment (for example transmembrane-helix contacts only).

EFNA3 ↔ GPR21 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.64 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel in every database checked (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and the fold is partner-specific (confident against 1 of 9 receptors tested, ipTM 0.64, interface PAE 2.13 A). However, EFNA3 (P52797) is a GPI-anchored glycoprotein (1 lipidation site, 3 glycosylation sites) and GPR21 (Q99679) is a glycosylated 7-TM orphan GPCR (2 glycosylation sites). The 587-token complex equals the two full-length precursors (238 + 349 residues), so the fold appears to include EFNA3's signal peptide and GPI propeptide, which are cleaved from the mature protein. A buried surface of 5310 A^2 with 1124 contacts is far more than a GPCR's small extracellular face can offer a roughly 200-residue ephrin ectodomain. My inference, not confirmed by a fold, is that hydrophobic precursor segments or glycan-bearing regions are packed against the transmembrane bundle, an interface that would not exist in vivo.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists EFNA3 with the keywords Glycoprotein, GPI-anchor and Lipoprotein, and with 3 glycosylation sites, 1 lipidation site (the GPI attachment, which replaces the cleaved C-terminal propeptide) and 2 disulfide bonds. GPR21 carries the keyword Glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites, which for a GPCR are expected on the extracellular N-terminus or loops, the only surface an ephrin could reach. The PubMed PTM searches (11 hits for EFNA3, 1 for GPR21) returned nothing specifically about glycosylation; the titles concern miR-210-3p/EFNA3 signalling and plasmalogen signalling through orphan GPCRs. Whether the glycosylation sites or the cleaved signal and GPI propeptides sit on the modeled interface is unverified. The very large interface (5310 A^2) and its moderate interface pLDDT of 68.4 make this plausible.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for EFNA3 and GPR21 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for the pair. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR21 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. UniProt describes EFNA3's function as a ligand for Eph receptor tyrosine kinases, and GPR21 as an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
EPHA2
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was not available in this run. Recommended next steps: 1. Re-fold using mature EFNA3 only (signal peptide and C-terminal GPI propeptide removed) against GPR21, and check whether ipTM stays at or above 0.5. 2. Inspect the interface topology on GPR21. The contacts must be Extracellular, with or without the transmembrane boundary; any Cytoplasmic or lipid-facing transmembrane contacts would mark an artifact. 3. Check whether the 3 EFNA3 and 2 GPR21 N-glycosylation sites fall on the interface. If they do, re-fold with the glycans attached. 4. Check whether the modeled surface on EFNA3 overlaps its Eph-receptor-binding loop, for example by folding with EPHA2 as a competitor. EPHA2 is suggested as a canonical ephrin-A receptor from general knowledge, not from a tool result. 5. If the interface survives these checks, test binding at the bench with EFNA3-Fc on GPR21-expressing cells.

IGF2 ↔ GPRC5C IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the IGF2–GPRC5C interface sits on the cytoplasmic face of GPRC5C. IGF2 is a secreted hormone with no cytosolic surface, so it cannot physically meet that face. This undercuts my earlier compartment-plausibility argument, which assumed IGF2 would contact the extracellular side. The same fold also places a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled interface. AF3 does not represent glycans, so the bare-sequence contact is unreliable even if the interface were extracellular. I had left this as an unresolved caveat, but it now falls on the interface itself. The weak breadth-control signal (1 of 17 receptors, ipTM 0.49, interface pLDDT 64.7) does not offset a wrong-topology pose. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all show no prior evidence, so the pair is not known. Tissue co-expression plausibility alone does not rescue a pose on the wrong side of the membrane.
PTM / glycosylation
GPRC5C is a glycoprotein with 1 annotated glycosylation site. The fold places that site on the modeled interface, so a glycan-dependent or lipidation-dependent contact is likely not represented by AF3. The IGF2 UniProt lookup returned the wrong entry, so no PTM data was obtained for the real ligand.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for IGF2 and GPRC5C together.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING association, no CellPhoneDB entry (GPRC5C is absent from its gene table) and 0 PubMed co-occurrence papers. The pair is not already known.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Optionally re-fold IGF2 against the GPRC5C extracellular domain only, and check whether a pose on the extracellular face holds up, ideally with glycan-aware modeling.

GNRH2 ↔ MRGPRX2 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.65 · pTM
Rationale
The AF3 fold put GNRH2 in contact with both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues of MRGPRX2. A secreted ligand cannot touch both faces of an intact membrane receptor, so the modeled interface is physically invalid and cannot support CONFIRMED_NOVEL. The likely cause is that AF3 was given the 85-aa prohormone, whose GAP region and signal peptide are not part of the bioactive ligand. The real ligand is the processed, amidated decapeptide. Other evidence is unchanged: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence are all negative, and HPA rates the pair plausible (secreted hormone versus mast-cell membrane GPCR). MRGPRX2 is also a promiscuous receptor for cationic, amphipathic peptides, and the GnRH-II decapeptide fits that profile, so the hypothesis is still reasonable. It rests on pharmacophore reasoning only and has no valid structural support, so I am downgrading to INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE rather than IMPLAUSIBLE.
PTM / glycosylation
Neither protein has glycosylation sites. GNRH2 is C-terminally amidated and proteolytically processed, with an N-terminal pyroglutamate in the mature peptide. A bare prohormone fold does not represent this and may explain the spurious cytoplasmic/extracellular contacts.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence count = 0; no papers mention both genes.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found=false, CellPhoneDB found=false, PubMed co-occurrence 0 hits. There is no prior literature on this pair, so it remains novel in the literature sense (known_interaction_strength=none).
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold MRGPRX2 with only the mature GnRH-II decapeptide (pGlu-HWSHGWYPG-NH2), representing the pyroGlu and C-terminal amidation where possible. Then check that the interface is restricted to the extracellular vestibule and TM-bundle orthosteric pocket. Compare against known MRGPRX2 peptide agonist poses (for example substance P or cortistatin-14).

Basic secretagogues comprise a set of cationic amphiphilic drugs, as well as endo- or exogenous peptides, consisting of a basic head group and a hydrophobic core (PubMed:25517090). MRGPRX2 is both coupled to G(q) and G(i) G proteins: G(q) coupling activates phospholipase C-beta, releasing diacylglycerol (DAG) and inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate (IP3) second messengers, while G(i) coupling mediates inhibition of adenylate cyclase activity (PubMed:28288109, PubMed:34789874, PubMed:34789875). The inflammatory response can then activate or sensitize nociceptors, promoting pain (By similarity). GHRL ↔ MRGPRX4 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.65 · pTM
Rationale
GHRL's UniProt entry (Q9UBU3) states that octanoylated ghrelin-28 is the predominant form and that "octanoylation is essential for the activation of the growth hormone secretagogue receptor type 1 (GHSR)" — the protein carries 3 Lipidation features and the keyword "Lipoprotein", a lipid-mediated binding mechanism AF3's bare-sequence fold cannot represent at all, so the modeled ipTM=0.65 interface is of an unacylated peptide that in vivo would present a very different pharmacophore. MRGPRX4 (Q96LA9) is moreover documented as a receptor for a chemically unrelated ligand class — bile acids, with a preference for 3-sulfated bile acids such as deoxycholate 3-sulfate — not for peptide hormones, and it carries 2 N-glycosylation sites on its extracellular face where a peptide would have to dock. No prior literature or database links the pair.
PTM / glycosylation
GHRL UniProt ptm_topology_feature_counts: Lipidation 3, Modified residue 1; keywords include "Lipoprotein" and "Amidation"; FUNCTION explicitly makes Ser3 octanoylation essential for receptor activation. MRGPRX4 UniProt: Glycosylation 2, Disulfide bond 2, keyword "Glycoprotein" — the glycans sit on the extracellular/loop face that any peptide ligand would contact. PubMed PTM search for GHRL returned 5 mostly irrelevant hits (tau neuropathology, chicken bursal B-cells); for MRGPRX4, 2 hits including PMID 38718132 on phospho-drug-associated pruritus, i.e. its ligand recognition is sensitive to ligand chemical modification state. AF3 modeled neither the octanoyl chain nor the receptor glycans.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for GHRL-MRGPRX4 (found=false, no channels). CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (found=false); MRGPRX4 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 hits. So the pair is genuinely unreported — novelty is not the issue here, mechanism is.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-run AF3 (or AlphaFold3 with CCD-defined modified residue) with ghrelin's Ser3 n-octanoyl group represented and with N-glycans at MRGPRX4's two annotated sites, and check whether the interface survives and whether a glycosylation site lands on the modeled contact surface — I could not run fold_complex/resolve_ptm_artifact in this run. Also check the pose location: a genuine MRGPRX4 agonist contacts the transmembrane bundle (bile-acid orthosteric pocket), so a ghrelin peptide resting only on extracellular loops would be a negative result. Wet-lab test: Ca2+/IP1 assay of acyl- and des-acyl-ghrelin on MRGPRX4-expressing cells versus the bile-acid positive control deoxycholate 3-sulfate.

FGA ↔ GPR101 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.53 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows two problems that my earlier LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX verdict ignored. (1) Topology violation: the AF3 interface is on GPR101's cytoplasmic face, but FGA is a secreted plasma protein with no cytosolic localisation and no membrane topology. A secreted fibrinogen chain cannot physically reach the intracellular side of a plasma-membrane GPCR, so the predicted contact is geometrically impossible in vivo. (2) A glycosylation/lipidation site lies directly on the modeled interface. AF3 does not represent these modifications, so the contact is likely a bare-sequence artifact. This is secondary to the topology problem, and I had previously treated it as an unresolved caveat. The earlier missing-subunit argument (FGA is normally part of the FGA/FGB/FGG hexamer) still applies, but it is now a minor point. Supporting evidence is also weak: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all show no record of the pair (known_interaction_strength=none), and ipTM is 0.53 with interface pLDDT 68.8. HPA compartment plausibility ('plausible') only means the proteins could meet extracellularly. It does not rescue an interface modeled on the cytoplasmic face. I am therefore downgrading to IMPLAUSIBLE at medium confidence, since the topology flag comes from a single automated fold analysis.
PTM / glycosylation
FGA is glycosylated and phosphorylated, and GPR101 is glycosylated. The fold flags a glycosylation/lipidation site directly on the modeled interface, which AF3 cannot represent. This further undermines the predicted contact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for FGA and GPR101 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 hits) all show no record of FGA-GPR101. GPR101 is an orphan receptor. known_interaction_strength=none.
Other subunits
FGB;FGG
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is of interest, re-model the extracellular domain of GPR101 with the full fibrinogen hexamer (FGA, FGB, FGG) and with glycans. Check whether the interface moves to an extracellular face.

FGFR1 ↔ GPR50 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.38 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the modeled FGFR1–GPR50 interface covers both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That is topologically impossible across an intact membrane, so the large 7088 Å² interface (ipTM 0.38, interface pLDDT 64.7) is probably an artifact of folding two membrane proteins without a bilayer. Low interface PAE does not fix this. The check also found a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the interface, and FGFR1's extracellular glycans are not represented in AF3, so any extracellular contact is unreliable too. The pair has no literature, STRING or CellPhoneDB support, and HPA plausibility is unknown. The only positive points are that both are plasma-membrane receptors and that GPR50 binds TGFBR1, which is an analogy and not evidence for FGFR1. I am moving from INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE to IMPLAUSIBLE because the only structural evidence is internally inconsistent and nothing independent supports the pair. Confidence is medium because a real interaction, such as a transmembrane or cytoplasmic one, is not excluded. It is simply not shown by this fold.
PTM / glycosylation
FGFR1 has 8 extracellular glycosylation sites. The fold check places a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled interface, and AF3 does not represent glycans, so the modeled contact cannot be trusted.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for FGFR1 and GPR50 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 papers) all report nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength=none). GPR50's binding to TGFBR1 is only an analogy.
Other subunits
KL
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold only the plausible same-side segments, such as the FGFR1 and GPR50 cytoplasmic or transmembrane regions, or fold with a membrane-aware method. Test experimentally with co-IP or proximity ligation in pituitary or lactotroph cells.

WNT3 ↔ GPR101 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.55 · pTM
Rationale
WNT3 (secreted) and the orphan GPCR GPR101 (cell membrane per UniProt) are a compartment-plausible ligand-receptor pair with no prior record in STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed (0 co-mentions), so the pair is novel. However, WNT3 is a lipoprotein and glycoprotein (UniProt P56703: 1 lipidation site, 2 glycosylation sites), and GPR101 (Q96P66) carries 2 glycosylation sites; Wnt receptor engagement is known to depend on the lipid adduct, which AF3's bare-sequence model does not represent. Combined with a marginal ipTM of 0.55, interface pLDDT of 67.7 and p_twoway of 0.0528, the modelled interface cannot be trusted as drawn. No fold was available in this run to check whether the modification sites sit on the interface.
PTM / glycosylation
WNT3 (UniProt P56703): keywords Glycoprotein and Lipoprotein; feature counts are 1 lipidation, 2 glycosylation and 11 disulfide bonds. The PubMed PTM search returned 52 hits, including PMID 38994173, "N-glycosylation of Wnt3 regulates the progression of hepatocellular carcinoma by affecting Wnt/β-catenin signal pathway". GPR101 (UniProt Q96P66): keyword Glycoprotein; 2 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond; the PubMed PTM search returned 1 hit, which is not about GPR101 glycosylation. The WNT3 lipid adduct and the glycans on both proteins are extracellular and plausibly relevant to an extracellular ligand-receptor interface, and none of them is represented in the AF3 model. Whether any of these sites lies on the modelled interface is unverified.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for WNT3 and GPR101 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for WNT3 and GPR101. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR101 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. There is no prior report of this pair. UniProt describes WNT3 as a ligand for frizzled-family seven-transmembrane receptors and GPR101 as an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
FZD family receptor (one representative, e.g. FZD8);LRP6
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Run an AF3 fold of WNT3 with GPR101 and inspect the structural analysis: confirm the interface falls on GPR101 extracellular or transmembrane-boundary residues (not cytoplasmic), and check whether the WNT3 lipidation site, the 2 WNT3 N-glycosylation sites or the 2 GPR101 glycosylation sites lie on the interface. 2) If a glycosylation site is on the interface, re-fold with the core glycan attached; also re-fold with the WNT3 lipid modification represented. 3) As a specificity control, compare against WNT3 folded with one frizzled receptor (its known receptor class), with and without the co-receptor LRP6. The supplied breadth data (1 of 5 receptors tested was confident) argues against a fully non-specific WNT3 surface, but p_twoway of 0.0528 is borderline. 4) Wet-lab: a binding assay using properly lipidated, secreted WNT3 on GPR101-expressing cells, and a GPR101 Gs/cAMP readout.

CADM4 ↔ GPR37 LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX low confidence ipTM 0.47 · pTM
Rationale
The real AF3 fold flagged a topology violation: the modelled A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which cannot happen across an intact membrane. That undermines the structural support. Together with the earlier ipTM of 0.47 and the near-clash distance of 1.77 A, the pose is probably a bare-sequence artifact and not a real contact, so I withdraw CONFIRMED_NOVEL. The biological case is unchanged: both proteins are brain and oligodendrocyte membrane proteins linked to myelination, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed return nothing for the pair. Because the case rests on co-expression and shared function and not on the pose, this is a possible indirect association within a myelin-associated surface complex (CADM4 with CADM2/3 and MAG) and not a validated direct interaction. The 3 glycosylation sites on each protein add further uncertainty, since AF3 does not model glycans.
PTM / glycosylation
CADM4 and GPR37 each have 3 annotated glycosylation sites, mostly extracellular. CADM4 is also palmitoylated. AF3 models none of these, so any extracellular ectodomain contact could differ from the bare-protein pose.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 papers mentioning CADM4 and GPR37 together.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 papers) all returned nothing for this pair, so it is not known. Known-interaction strength is none.
Other subunits
CADM2;CADM3;MAG
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-run the fold with CADM2, CADM3 and MAG as extra subunits, restricted to the extracellular domains. Check that any interface is topologically valid, meaning extracellular-only or transmembrane-compatible. Consider an experimental co-IP in oligodendrocytes.

ROR2 ↔ MRGPRF IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.66 · pTM
Rationale
I'm downgrading from CONFIRMED_NOVEL for two reasons. (1) Both proteins sit in the plasma membrane, but HPA shows no shared tissue-level protein expression, and neither is secreted. The bone-marrow MSC RNA overlap I relied on is RNA-only, so it doesn't show the proteins meet in the same cell. Without protein-level co-expression, I have no substantiated route for contact. (2) The real AF3 fold's 6079 Å^2 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That is topologically impossible across an intact membrane, so the confident-looking pose (ipTM 0.66, interface pLDDT 60) is a bare-sequence artifact, not a real contact. The pair still has no STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed trace, so it isn't a known interaction. But the lack of prior evidence plus no co-expression plus a topology-violating fold leaves no support for a real interaction.
PTM / glycosylation
ROR2 has 3 glycosylation sites and MRGPRF has 1. Neither is a lectin. Glycosylation is a minor caveat here, and the topology violation is the main problem.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for ROR2 and MRGPRF returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING association, no CellPhoneDB curated interaction (MRGPRF isn't in the gene table), and 0 PubMed co-occurrence papers. The pair is not known, but absence of prior evidence gives no positive support either.
Other subunits
WNT5A
Follow-up (not yet executed): Folding tools were not available in this run. Recommended next steps: (1) Re-fold ROR2 + MRGPRF and read the interface topology for both chains, to confirm the 6079 A^2 interface does not combine Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues at once (AF3 models no lipid bilayer, so a large interface between a single-pass RTK and a 7TM GPCR is at risk of this), and to check whether any of the 3 ROR2 or 1 MRGPRF N-glycosylation sites sit on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. (2) Counter-screen for specificity: only 1 partner receptor was tested (1 of 1 confident), so fold ROR2 against two or three unrelated class A GPCRs, including other MRGPR family members, and fold MRGPRF against ROR1 and an unrelated single-pass RTK. (3) Optionally fold with WNT5A, the ROR2 ligand named in UniProt (CellPhoneDB lists no curated complex for ROR2, so this is a suggestion and not a known missing subunit). (4) Wet-lab: co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in bone marrow MSCs or smooth muscle cells, where both are expressed.

LGALS3 ↔ GPR39 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.53 · pTM
Rationale
Compartment and novelty checks support the pair: LGALS3 is secreted and at the plasma membrane, GPR39 is a cell-surface GPCR, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed show no known interaction. However, UniProt describes Galectin-3 (P17931) as a "Galactose-specific lectin" and GPR39 (O43194) as a Glycoprotein with 3 annotated glycosylation sites, so any real galectin-3 binding to GPR39 would most likely be glycan-mediated, which AF3's bare-sequence model cannot represent. The fold itself is only borderline (ipTM 0.53, interface pLDDT 66.8), so the modeled protein-protein interface is not reliable evidence of a real contact surface.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt describes LGALS3 (P17931) as a "Galactose-specific lectin" with the keyword Lectin, meaning its documented binding mechanism to cell-surface partners is through beta-galactoside glycans. UniProt lists GPR39 (O43194) with the keyword Glycoprotein and 3 annotated Glycosylation features; for a GPCR these are expected on the extracellular N-terminus or loops, although the per-residue positions were not checked. This is directly relevant to the interface: the real contact would be lectin-to-glycan, and the glycans could also sterically occupy the modeled protein surface. The PubMed PTM searches (39 hits for LGALS3, 25 for GPR39) returned mostly non-specific titles and added nothing site-specific.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed returned 1 co-mention (PMID 40616194, Clin Epigenetics 2025, DNA methylation and transcriptional profiling in bronchopulmonary dysplasia). This looks unrelated to a direct interaction between the two proteins.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for LGALS3 and GPR39. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence returned 1 hit (PMID 40616194, a methylation and transcriptional profiling study of hyperoxia-induced bronchopulmonary dysplasia), which is a co-mention in an omics gene list and not a report of binding. The pair is therefore unreported as a direct interaction.
Other subunits
HTR1A;GALR1
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold could be run in this triage, so three checks remain. (1) Re-fold LGALS3 with GPR39 and confirm whether the interface sits on GPR39's extracellular residues and whether any of the 3 UniProt N-glycosylation sites lie on it; if so, re-fold with the core glycan attached. (2) Inspect the supplied model for clashes, since the minimum separation of 2.21 A with 863 contacts and a buried surface of 3905 A^2 is suspiciously tight for an ipTM of only 0.53 and an interface pLDDT of 66.8. (3) Test at the bench whether galectin-3 binding to GPR39-expressing cells is abolished by lactose or a galectin-3 CRD inhibitor, or by PNGase F treatment or glycosylation-site mutants of GPR39; this would separate glycan-dependent binding from a true protein-protein contact. The supplied breadth screen (1 of 5 partner receptors confident, p_twoway = 0.033) suggests some specificity to GPR39, which makes the glycan-dependent hypothesis worth testing and not simply discarding. HTR1A and GALR1 are listed as other subunits only because UniProt reports GPR39 forms heteroreceptor complexes with them; CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein and there is no evidence they are required for this pair.

GFRA3 ↔ GPR37L1 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.51 · pTM
Rationale
I'm changing the verdict from LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM to IMPLAUSIBLE, because the contradiction is valid and the PTM explanation was the weaker of the two concerns. Neither GFRA3 nor GPR37L1 is secreted, and HPA shows zero shared protein-level tissue or cell-type expression, so the data give no route for the two to meet. The fold check also flagged a topology violation, and the AF3 support is already borderline (ipTM 0.51, interface pLDDT 65.4, 2.0 Å minimum separation). The 3488 Å^2 interface is probably a bare-sequence modeling artifact. Glycosylation on both proteins (3 sites on GFRA3, 6 on GPR37L1) could be a secondary confound, but it isn't needed to explain the result. There is also no prior evidence for the pair: STRING and CellPhoneDB returned nothing and PubMed co-occurrence found 0 papers. That absence supports novelty only in the sense that nobody has reported it. It doesn't offset the lack of any co-localization.
PTM / glycosylation
GFRA3 has 3 glycosylation sites plus a GPI anchor. GPR37L1 has 6 glycosylation sites, and its N-terminus is MMP-processed (PMID 33203955). Glycans are a possible confound for the interface, but whether any site sits on it was not established. This is secondary to the topology and co-localization problems.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for GFRA3 and GPR37L1 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. The pair is unreported, but with no co-expression this doesn't make it plausible.
Other subunits
RET;ARTN
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is still of interest, test it with RET included and with membrane topology constraints. First look for a cell type or tissue where both proteins are actually expressed.

PTN ↔ GPR22 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.45 · pTM
Rationale
PTN (secreted growth factor) and GPR22 (orphan Gi/Go GPCR) are a compartment-plausible ligand/receptor pair, both brain-expressed, with no prior report in STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed (0 co-mentions), so the pair is novel. However, UniProt describes PTN's receptor binding as mediated by chondroitin sulfate glycan chains on proteoglycan receptors, and GPR22 carries 2 annotated glycosylation sites and the Glycoprotein keyword; AF3 models neither. The structural support is also marginal: ipTM 0.45 is below the 0.5 convention despite interface PAE 2.67 A and interface pLDDT 70.9, and the minimum separation of 1.82 A suggests an un-relaxed, near-clashing interface.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt PTN (P21246): the function text states it "binds cell-surface proteoglycan receptor via their chondroitin sulfate (CS) groups" and it carries the Heparin-binding keyword. Its documented receptor-binding mechanism is therefore largely glycosaminoglycan-mediated, which a bare-sequence AF3 fold cannot represent, although UniProt also notes non-proteoglycan receptors such as ALK. PTN itself has no annotated glycosylation (5 disulfide bonds only). UniProt GPR22 (Q99680): Glycoprotein keyword and 2 annotated glycosylation sites, which were not modeled; whether they lie on the modeled interface (buried surface area 2427 A2) is unknown. The pubmed_ptm_glycosylation searches were uninformative: 84 hits for PTN and 2 for GPR22, with none of the returned titles addressing glycosylation of either protein.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for PTN together with GPR22.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (GPR22 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. No hit in any of the three sources, so the pair is not previously reported. GPR22 is an orphan receptor per UniProt.
Other subunits
PTPRZ1;SDC3
Follow-up (not yet executed): A fold could not be run in this triage. Recommended: (1) re-fold PTN + GPR22 with AF3 and read the interface topology, to confirm the contact is on GPR22's extracellular N-terminus/loops and not its cytoplasmic side, and to check whether either of the 2 GPR22 glycosylation sites sits on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the core N-glycan attached. (2) Counter-screen PTN against unrelated GPCRs, because PTN is a highly basic heparin-binding protein prone to non-specific electrostatic contacts; the supplied breadth data (1 of 4 receptors confident, p_twoway = 0.033) argue for some specificity but come from only 4 receptors. (3) Inspect the 1.82 A minimum separation for clashes. (4) If the pose survives, test in the wet lab with PTN stimulation of GPR22-expressing cells using a Gi/Go cAMP-inhibition readout, with and without chondroitinase/heparinase treatment to separate protein-mediated from glycosaminoglycan-mediated binding. PTPRZ1 and SDC3 are listed as known PTN proteoglycan co-receptors from UniProt, not as CellPhoneDB complex members (no CellPhoneDB complex was found for either protein).

NMS ↔ GPR158 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.56 · pTM
Rationale
NMS is a secreted brain neuropeptide and GPR158 is a brain-enriched plasma-membrane GPCR, so a paracrine ligand-receptor pairing is compartmentally plausible, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 papers) all show no prior record of this pair. Support from the fold itself is only marginal: ipTM is 0.56 and interface pLDDT is 56.0, although interface PAE is 2.69 A and only 1 of the 3 receptors NMS was tested against passed the confidence gate. No fold could be run here, so it is unchecked whether the interface is on the extracellular side or whether any of GPR158's 5 glycosylation sites sits on it; the very large interface (5202 A^2, 1117 contacts) also suggests the full NMS precursor was folded and not the mature amidated peptide.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates GPR158 as a Glycoprotein with 5 glycosylation sites, 2 disulfide bonds and 6 modified residues (Phosphoprotein, Ubl conjugation). Glycosylation sites on a GPCR are expected to be extracellular, which is where a secreted peptide would bind, so a glycan at the modeled interface is possible but could not be checked without a fold. NMS carries the keywords Amidation and Cleavage on pair of basic residues, with 1 modified residue: the active form is a processed, C-terminally amidated peptide that AF3 did not represent. The PubMed PTM search for NMS (61 hits) was dominated by unrelated uses of the abbreviation (e.g. native mass spectrometry) and is uninformative; the 3 hits for GPR158 concern its neuronal function, not its glycosylation.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for NMS and GPR158 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for NMS-GPR158. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR158 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. UniProt describes GPR158 as a metabotropic receptor for glycine (also taurine) and a possible receptor for osteocalcin (BGLAP), with GPC4 as a binding partner; NMS is not among its documented ligands.
Other subunits
RGS7;GNB5
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold was run in this triage. Recommended: (1) re-fold GPR158 with the mature amidated NMS peptide instead of the full precursor, and check that the interface residues are on GPR158's extracellular side and not across both cytoplasmic and extracellular regions; (2) check whether any of GPR158's 5 UniProt glycosylation sites falls on the modeled interface, and if so re-fold with the glycan attached; (3) optionally fold GPR158 as its homodimer, with or without RGS7-GNB5 (the intracellular complex UniProt says it recruits), to see whether the peptide site persists; (4) wet-lab: test mature NMS on GPR158-expressing cells using a binding assay or an RGS7-GAP/cAMP readout, with the established NMS receptors NMUR1/NMUR2 (from general knowledge, not retrieved by these tools) as positive controls and glycine as the GPR158 reference ligand.
Validation strategy: Stimulation assay (Mature amidated NMS-23/NMS-33 peptide vs. precursor-derived constructs, tested on GPR158 with and without co-expressed RGS7–Gβ5; optionally compare WT vs. glycosylation-site mutant GPR158)
Why this method
NMS is a secreted neuropeptide and GPR158 is a plasma-membrane GPCR, so a paracrine ligand–receptor pair is best tested functionally by applying the ligand to receptor-expressing cells. GPR158 is an orphan, atypical class C GPCR that signals through RGS7–Gβ5 and modulates cAMP, so a cAMP readout is informative. Co-IP or PLA on a soluble peptide would be weak.
Target tissue(s)
brain (cerebral cortex), brain (hypothalamus/SCN region, NMS-expressing neurons), retina
Why these tissues (HPA)
HPA shows GPR158 is brain-enriched (10.1 nTPM brain, 4.2 retina) and has protein-level expression in cerebral cortex. NMS RNA is brain-specific (1.0 nTPM, secreted in brain; 1.6 nCPM in other brain neurons). Brain is the only real overlap, and the low NMS level suggests a restricted neuronal population. Retina is a GPR158-positive secondary tissue.
Cell type(s)
Other brain neurons (cortical neurons), Retinal bipolar cells (GPR158-expressing)
Protocol notes
1) Use HEK293 cells (or primary cortical neurons) transfected with GPR158 + RGS7 + Gβ5 and a cAMP biosensor (GloSensor or BRET-EPAC). Stimulate with synthetic amidated NMS-23 and NMS-33 over a dose range (1 nM–10 µM), with and without forskolin to detect Gi-type cAMP suppression. Also run a Gαo/Gαi BRET assay as a second readout. 2) Run parallel arms with a precursor-length NMS construct, since AF3 probably folded the full precursor, and with a peptide-processing-competent co-culture (NMS-secreting cells). 3) Add binding evidence: a labelled NMS peptide (e.g. biotin or fluorescent) in a cell-surface binding assay on GPR158-expressing vs. parental cells. Confirm surface expression of GPR158 by an N-terminal tag. 4) Test NMUR1 and NMUR2 as the known NMS receptors for assay validity and selectivity.
Controls
Positive: NMS on NMUR1/NMUR2-expressing cells (Gq calcium response), which validates the peptide. Assay controls: forskolin for the cAMP window. Negative: parental/mock-transfected cells, GPR158 without RGS7–Gβ5, scrambled or non-amidated NMS peptide, an unrelated neuropeptide (e.g. NPY), and GPR158 knockdown neurons.
Caveats
Confidence is low: ipTM is 0.56 and interface pLDDT is 56. The 5202 A^2 interface suggests the full NMS precursor was modelled, not the mature amidated NMS-23/33 peptide, so the predicted interface may not exist for the real ligand. The fold was not checked for extracellular-side placement, and GPR158 has 5 glycosylation sites that may block or gate binding, so the glycosylation-site mutant arm is worth running. GPR158 may need the RGS7–Gβ5 partners, and it is an atypical orphan receptor, so it may give no classical agonist response. A negative cAMP result would not fully exclude binding, so use a binding assay too. NMS RNA is low in HPA (1.0 nTPM), and the lymphoid tissue protein signal for GPR158 may be antibody non-specificity.

GP1BB ↔ GPR63 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.58 · pTM
Rationale
The real AF3 fold shows the A-B interface spanning both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which is impossible across an intact membrane for two plasma-membrane proteins. My original LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX verdict did not address this. The ipTM of 0.58 is modest, so the contact is most likely a bare-sequence artifact with no consistent topology. The earlier concerns still apply, but they now matter less: GP1BB is an obligate GP1BA/GP1BB/GPIX/GPV subunit, and both proteins carry glycans that AF3 does not model. The evidence for a real interaction is also thin. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing (known_interaction_strength=none), and the only support is RNA overlap in choroid plexus epithelium. HPA plausibility is unknown, so it offers nothing to offset the topology problem. I therefore changed the verdict to IMPLAUSIBLE rather than keeping LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX.
PTM / glycosylation

CEACAM1 ↔ MRGPRX3 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.59 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature source reports this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-mention count 0), and both proteins are cell-membrane proteins per UniProt, so an interaction is topologically possible and would be novel. However, CEACAM1 (P13688) carries 20 annotated glycosylation sites on its extracellular Ig domains, which AF3's bare-sequence model does not represent, and the modeled interface is very large (buried surface area 3024 Å2, 524 contacts) at only moderate confidence (ipTM 0.59, interface pLDDT 69.4), so it plausibly covers surface that is glycan-shielded in vivo. This could not be verified per residue because no fold was available in this run, and expression overlap between the two proteins is not supported by HPA, hence low confidence.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates CEACAM1 (P13688) as a Glycoprotein with 20 glycosylation sites, plus 3 disulfide bonds and 4 modified residues; the glycosylation sites are on the extracellular Ig domains. The PubMed PTM/glycosylation search returned 112 papers for CEACAM1, including "Conformational Preferences for N-Glycans at the Surface of CEACAM1-Ig1" (PMID 41416709) and an N-glycoproteomic profiling study (PMID 42958405), confirming heavy N-glycosylation of the ectodomain. MRGPRX3 (Q96LB0) has no annotated glycosylation or lipidation sites in UniProt and 0 PubMed PTM hits. Relevance to the modeled interface is plausible but unverified: if the 3024 Å2 interface involves the CEACAM1 ectodomain, it very likely overlaps glycan-covered surface; if it is a transmembrane helix-to-helix contact, glycosylation would not be the concern. No per-residue interface data were available to distinguish these.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CEACAM1 and MRGPRX3 returned a count of 0 with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CEACAM1 and MRGPRX3. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and MRGPRX3 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. The supplied breadth screen shows CEACAM1's partner was confident against 1 of 7 receptors tested (threshold interface PAE <= 3.75 Å, no clash; p_twoway = 0.049197), so the prediction is not a generic sticky-partner result, but that is fold specificity only, not binding evidence.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Not run here because folding tools were unavailable: re-fold CEACAM1 with MRGPRX3 and inspect the interface residues and membrane topology, specifically (1) whether any of the 20 CEACAM1 N-glycosylation sites sit on the interface, and if so re-fold with core glycans attached at those sites; (2) whether the interface mixes cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which would be physically impossible; (3) whether the contact is mainly between the CEACAM1 transmembrane helix and the MRGPRX3 helical bundle, a common AF3 membrane-protein artifact. Before any bench work, confirm that the two proteins are actually co-expressed or in contact in a relevant tissue, for example dorsal root ganglion sensory neurons or skin, since HPA shows no overlap. CEACAM1 forms homophilic dimers through its N-terminal Ig domain, so a CEACAM1 homodimer competition control would also be informative. A CORUM check for larger complexes was not possible.

TNFRSF8 ↔ GPR65 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.66 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows two problems. (1) Topology violation: the modeled TNFRSF8–GPR65 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. A single contact cannot do that across an intact membrane, so the geometry is physically impossible. The moderate ipTM (0.66) and low interface pLDDT (68.8) therefore reflect a bare-sequence model that ignores the membrane, not a real binding mode. (2) A glycosylation/lipidation site sits directly on the modeled interface. Both proteins are glycoproteins (4 sites on TNFRSF8, 3 on GPR65), and AF3 folded them without glycans, so glycan steric effects could not be represented. The earlier 'confirmed novel' call did not account for either finding. The lack of any STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed record (known_interaction_strength = none) still means the pair is not already known. HPA shows no shared cell type (plausibility unknown), so nothing supports co-occurrence. The absence of prior evidence does not rescue a topologically impossible, glycan-confounded interface. I classify this as an artifact of the bare-sequence model, not a validated novel interaction.
PTM / glycosylation
Both are UniProt glycoproteins (TNFRSF8 has 4 glycosylation sites, GPR65 has 3). The fold flagged a glycosylation/lipidation site directly on the modeled interface. AF3 does not model glycans, so the modeled contact may not be real.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 papers mentioning both TNFRSF8 and GPR65.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association. CellPhoneDB: no match (GPR65 is not in the gene table, so the miss is uninformative for it). PubMed co-mention: 0. Known-interaction strength is none, so the pair is not a known interaction.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Do not pursue the current pose. If retested, restrict the model to the extracellular domains only, include glycans on the annotated N-glycosylation sites, and check that the interface sits on one side of the membrane.

BMP6 ↔ GPR31 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
The flagged fold analysis changes my verdict. The AF3 A-B interface spans both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues of GPR31. A secreted ligand like BMP6 cannot touch both sides of an intact membrane, so the modeled pose is not physically realizable. This agrees with my earlier note of steric interpenetration (1.55 Å minimum separation, 1092 contacts, ipTM 0.49, interface pLDDT 55.8), which I had wrongly treated as only "not excluded". The second flag is a glycosylation or lipidation site sitting directly on the modeled interface. BMP6 has 5 annotated glycosylation sites and GPR31 has 1. AF3 does not represent these modifications, so the contact surface is unreliable and cannot rescue the pose. Nothing else supports the pair. STRING and CellPhoneDB found nothing, PubMed co-mentions are 0, and GPR31 is a lipid-sensing GPCR with no protein-ligand precedent, while BMP6 has its own receptors. HPA plausibility (co-expression and compatible topology) only shows the two proteins could meet. It does not outweigh a topologically impossible, low-confidence model. I therefore move from INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE to IMPLAUSIBLE, with medium confidence because the topology failure applies to this model rather than ruling out every possible binding mode.
PTM / glycosylation
BMP6 has 5 glycosylation sites and GPR31 has 1. The fold check now shows a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled interface, which AF3 cannot represent. This undermines the modeled contact surface.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for BMP6 and GPR31 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING association, no CellPhoneDB entry (GPR31 is absent from the gene table), and 0 PubMed co-mentions. known_interaction_strength=none, so the pair is unreported, not known.
Other subunits
ACVR1;ACVR2B;BMPR1A;HJV
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is still of interest, test it with an experiment, such as a binding assay of mature BMP6 against GPR31-expressing cells. Alternatively, refold the mature BMP6 dimer against the GPR31 extracellular face only, and compare with ACVR1 and ACVR2B as positive controls.

GHRL ↔ MRGPRX1 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.63 · pTM
Rationale
GHRL is a secreted blood hormone (HPA "Predicted secreted proteins", secretome_function Hormone) and MRGPRX1 is a plasma-membrane GPCR, so a secreted-peptide/receptor pairing is compartmentally plausible and no coexpression in the same cell is required. However, UniProt (Q9UBU3) states that octanoylated ghrelin-28 is the predominant form and that "octanoylation is essential for the activation of the growth hormone secretagogue receptor type 1 (GHSR)" (PubMed:11306336, 35027551), with 3 annotated Lipidation features plus Amidation — a lipid modification central to ghrelin's receptor-binding mechanism that AF3's bare-sequence model cannot represent at all, so the modeled ipTM=0.63 interface is of an unacylated peptide that is not the physiologically active species. MRGPRX1 is additionally a notoriously polyspecific receptor for diverse cationic peptides (BAM22/BAM8-22, tick defensins, conotoxin CNF-Tx2, chloroquine), and this candidate was confident against 3 of 11 receptors tested, so a generic cationic-peptide-in-pocket pose is an expected non-specific outcome.
PTM / glycosylation
GHRL UniProt ptm_topology_feature_counts: Lipidation 3, Modified residue 1; keywords include "Lipoprotein" and "Amidation". The FUNCTION text explicitly makes Ser3 octanoylation essential for receptor activation — a mechanism-level modification AF3 does not model. MRGPRX1 carries keyword "Glycoprotein" with 1 Glycosylation site and 2 disulfide bonds; the single N-glycan is a lesser concern (disulfides are modeled fine), but it sits on the extracellular face where a peptide ligand would approach. The PubMed PTM searches were largely off-target (GHRL hits concern melatonin, frailty biomarkers, GH receptor glycosylation; MRGPRX1 hits concern itch/TRPV1 signalling), so UniProt is the informative source here.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found (found=false). CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (found=false), and MRGPRX1 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence for GHRL and MRGPRX1: 0 hits. By the novelty criteria this pair is genuinely unreported.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-run AF3 with the ghrelin Ser3 n-octanoyl group explicitly represented (as a covalent lipid modification or a CCD octanoyl ligand) and see whether the MRGPRX1 interface survives or shifts — the acylated peptide is the physiologically active species and the unmodified fold may not reflect it. In parallel, run a counter-screen folding ghrelin against 2-3 unrelated class-A peptide GPCRs plus GHSR as a positive control; MRGPRX1's polyspecificity for cationic peptides means specificity, not pose confidence, is the question. Wet-lab test: calcium-mobilisation/IP1 assay in MRGPRX1-transfected cells with acyl- and des-acyl-ghrelin, with BAM8-22 as positive control.

CCL11 ↔ GPR132 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.56 · pTM
Rationale
CCL11 is a secreted chemokine (HPA "Secreted to blood"; UniProt P51671) and GPR132 is a 7-transmembrane cell-membrane GPCR (UniProt Q9UNW8), so the pair is topologically plausible as a secreted-ligand/surface-receptor interaction. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 papers) all returned nothing, so it is unreported. Confidence is low because the fold is only marginal (ipTM 0.56, interface pLDDT 71.1, p_twoway 0.0499), GPR132 is annotated as a lipid/proton sensor rather than a chemokine receptor, CCL11's documented primary receptor is CCR3, and both proteins carry one glycosylation site whose position relative to the modeled interface was not checked.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists both proteins with the Glycoprotein keyword and 1 annotated glycosylation site each (CCL11: 1 glycosylation, 2 disulfide bonds; GPR132: 1 glycosylation, 1 disulfide bond). The PubMed PTM search returned 74 hits for CCL11 and 1 for GPR132, but the titles returned concern allergic inflammation and lactate-GPR132 signalling, not glycosylation of either protein. Whether either glycosylation site lies on the modeled interface is unknown. This is a caveat because GPR132's site would be on the extracellular face a chemokine contacts, but the mechanism is not described as glycan-mediated, so it does not on its own justify an artifact call.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CCL11 and GPR132 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association between CCL11 and GPR132. CellPhoneDB has no curated interaction for the pair, and GPR132 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. No source reports this pair. UniProt states CCL11 acts primarily through CCR3, and that GPR132 may be a receptor for oxidized free fatty acids such as 9-HODE and may have proton-sensing activity; neither entry mentions a chemokine-GPR132 pairing.
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold was run in this triage (fold tools unavailable). Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold CCL11 + GPR132 and read the interface topology to confirm that CCL11 contacts only Extracellular/Transmembrane residues of GPR132 and not Cytoplasmic ones, and check whether the single annotated glycosylation site of GPR132 (Q9UNW8) or of CCL11 (P51671) lies on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. (2) Fold CCL11 + CCR3 as a positive control to put ipTM 0.56 in context. (3) The supplied breadth screen has CCL11 confident against only 1 of 6 receptors tested, which supports specificity; the reverse counter-screen, GPR132 against other CC chemokines, would complete it. (4) Wet-lab: binding and Gq/calcium or beta-arrestin recruitment assay of recombinant CCL11 on GPR132-expressing cells, alongside the 9-HODE control.
Validation strategy: Stimulation assay
Why this method
CCL11 is a secreted chemokine (HPA: secreted to blood) and GPR132 is a 7TM GPCR, so a paracrine ligand-receptor model fits. A functional readout in GPR132-expressing cells tests whether CCL11 actually engages and signals through GPR132, which co-localization or co-IP could not show.
Target tissue(s)
Skin (fibroblast-rich dermis), Lung, Lymphoid tissue (spleen / lymph node), Bone marrow
Why these tissues (HPA)
HPA gives no tissue-level RNA or protein specificity for either gene. The only cell-type signal is CCL11 RNA in fibroblasts (44.2 nCPM), and GPR132 has no HPA cell-type enrichment. The tissues above are therefore chosen from outside the HPA data: fibroblast-rich connective tissue as the CCL11 source side, and immune/hematopoietic tissues as the plausible GPR132 side. The assay does not depend on tissue overlap, because it uses engineered cells and co-culture.
Cell type(s)
Fibroblasts (CCL11 source), Macrophages / monocytes (candidate GPR132-expressing responders), Eosinophils (CCR3-positive comparator)
Protocol notes
1) Build the receptor-expressing system: HEK293 or CHO cells stably expressing human GPR132, plus a parental/empty-vector line and a CCR3-expressing line as the positive control. Confirm surface expression of GPR132 by epitope tag (e.g. HA/FLAG) flow cytometry. 2) Stimulate with recombinant human CCL11 (0.1 nM–1 µM dose-response) and read out signalling that suits GPR132 (Gi/Gq/G13-coupled). Use a cAMP assay (forskolin-stimulated; GloSensor or HTRF), Ca2+ flux (Fluo-4 or aequorin with Gα16 co-expression), β-arrestin recruitment (PathHunter/NanoBiT) and pERK. Run a transwell migration assay on a GPR132-expressing immune line, e.g. a macrophage line or primary monocytes. 3) Orthogonal binding test: use tagged or fluorescent CCL11 for surface binding by flow cytometry in GPR132 versus parental cells, with competition by excess unlabeled CCL11. Optionally co-culture with CCL11-secreting primary dermal fibroblasts (induced with IL-4/TNF) and block with an anti-CCL11 antibody. 4) Rule out CCR3 and other receptors: use CCR3-negative cells, and test GPR132 knockdown/knockout in a responsive primary cell.
Controls
Positive: CCL11 on CCR3-expressing cells (Ca2+ flux, chemotaxis), and a known GPR132 agonist (9-HODE, or lactate/acidic pH) on the GPR132 line, to show the receptor is functional. Negative: parental cells, empty vector, heat-denatured CCL11, an unrelated chemokine (e.g. CCL2), anti-CCL11 neutralizing antibody, and pertussis toxin to define Gi dependence. Specificity: GPR132 knockout cells and a CCR3 antagonist (e.g. SB328437).
Caveats
Confidence is low: ipTM 0.56, interface pLDDT 71.1, p_twoway 0.05. GPR132 (G2A) is a lipid/proton-sensing GPCR, and CCL11's known receptor is CCR3, so a negative result is plausible. Both proteins have one glycosylation site whose position relative to the modeled interface was not checked, so the bare-sequence AF3 interface may be blocked in reality; consider glycosylation-site mutants or recombinant protein from mammalian cells rather than E. coli. HPA has no tissue-level expression for either protein, so tissue choice is inferred rather than data-driven. GPR132 is also constitutively active and responds to pH, so control buffer pH carefully. Recombinant chemokines can give non-specific effects at high concentrations.

GRIA4 ↔ GPR83 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.43 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the modeled GRIA4–GPR83 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. Two single-pass-or-polytopic membrane proteins cannot make that contact across an intact membrane, so the pose is topologically impossible. The same fold also puts a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the interface. Glycans or palmitoyl groups are not represented in AF3, so the modeled contact is probably not a real binding mode. This adds to the weak structural metrics: ipTM 0.43, interface pLDDT 63.2, and a PAE-gate pass against only 1 of 10 receptors. Novelty is not in doubt, since STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all show nothing for this pair. But novelty only means no one has reported it, not that it is real. Co-expression in neurons and shared cell-membrane annotation show the two proteins could meet, but they do not outweigh an impossible interface. I therefore move from CONFIRMED_NOVEL to IMPLAUSIBLE, with medium confidence because a topologically valid pose, such as an extracellular-only or intracellular-only contact, has not been tested.
PTM / glycosylation
The fold shows a glycosylation or lipidation site on the modeled interface. GRIA4 has 6 glycosylation sites and 2 lipidation sites (palmitoylation). GPR83 has 3 glycosylation sites. AF3 does not model these modifications, so the interface may be sterically blocked or otherwise unreal. Neither protein is a lectin.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for GRIA4 and GPR83 returned 0 papers.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING association, no CellPhoneDB curated interaction (GPR83 is absent from the gene table), and 0 PubMed co-occurrence papers. known_interaction_strength=none, so the pair is novel in the sense that nothing has been reported. It is not confirmed.
Other subunits
GRIA1;GRIA2;GRIA3
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is pursued, re-model only the extracellular domains (GRIA4 ATD/LBD with GPR83 N-terminus) or only the intracellular regions, ideally with the full AMPA-receptor tetramer and auxiliary subunits (e.g. TARPs) present. Check whether the contact is topologically valid and avoids the glycosylated and palmitoylated sites.

DSC2 ↔ GPR75 IMPLAUSIBLE low confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
DSC2 (desmocollin-2, a desmosomal cadherin) and GPR75 (a GPCR activated by CCL5 and 20-HETE) show no overlap in HPA-enriched expression: DSC2 is enhanced in esophagus and in suprabasal keratinocytes and esophageal/ocular epithelial cells, whereas GPR75 is enhanced in brain (9.8 nTPM) and retina (7.1 nTPM) and is "Not detected" at single-cell-type level. The AF3 ipTM of 0.48 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, interface pLDDT is only 62.3, and both proteins are N-glycosylated on their extracellular regions. Confidence is low because HPA lists only enriched tissues rather than a full expression matrix, and the supplied partner-breadth data do suggest specificity (interface PAE 3.01 A; only 1 of 9 receptors tested was confident; p_twoway=0.0147).
PTM / glycosylation
DSC2: UniProt keyword Glycoprotein with 5 annotated glycosylation sites, plus Phosphoprotein (3 modified residues) and propeptide cleavage; PMID 30942563 reports that incorporation of desmocollin-2 into the plasma membrane requires N-glycosylation at multiple sites, so its ectodomain is glycosylated in vivo. GPR75: UniProt keyword Glycoprotein with 3 annotated glycosylation sites; the PubMed PTM search returned 11 hits, but the titles shown concern 20-HETE signalling rather than GPR75 modification. If the modeled interface involves the extracellular regions, unmodeled glycans on both proteins could occlude it. Whether any site lies on the interface could not be checked without a fold.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for DSC2 and GPR75.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for DSC2-GPR75. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. The pair is unreported in all three sources, so it would be novel if real, but novelty alone does not support it.
Other subunits
DSG2;DSP;JUP;PKP2
Follow-up (not yet executed): The suggested other subunits are DSC2's desmosomal partners from general knowledge and the UniProt function text (DSG2, DSP), not from CellPhoneDB, which lists no complex for either protein; DSC2 has an HPA known-interactions count of 32, and CORUM was not available to check. If this candidate is pursued despite the expression mismatch: (1) Re-fold DSC2 + GPR75 with structural analysis to see whether the interface touches both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues at once, and whether any of the 5 DSC2 or 3 GPR75 N-glycosylation sites sit on it. The very large interface (buried surface area 6222 A^2, 1136 contacts, minimum separation 2.21 A) at only moderate interface pLDDT (62.3) and ipTM 0.48 could reflect a non-physical wrapping of the cadherin ectodomain around the receptor, but this has not been inspected. (2) Check a full quantitative expression matrix or spatial dataset for co-expression in retina/ocular epithelium or brain before any bench work. (3) If glycosylation sites fall on the interface, re-fold with glycans attached.

CD244 ↔ GPR34 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature source reports this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), so it is novel, and the supplied screen shows some specificity (1 of 7 partner receptors confident, p_twoway=0.045). However, both are heavily N-glycosylated plasma-membrane glycoproteins per UniProt (CD244 Q9BZW8: 8 glycosylation sites; GPR34 Q9UPC5: 5 glycosylation sites), the fold is only borderline (ipTM 0.57, interface pLDDT 66.5), and HPA shows no shared cell type (CD244 in NK cells and basophils; GPR34 in macrophages, microglia, Kupffer and Hofbauer cells). I could not run a fold in this run, so whether any glycosylation site actually lies on the modeled interface, and which side of the membrane the interface is on, is unverified; the call is a low-confidence artifact concern, not a demonstrated one.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates CD244 (Q9BZW8) as a Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein with 8 glycosylation sites, 4 modified residues and 1 disulfide bond; the glycosylation sites of a SLAM-family receptor lie on the extracellular Ig domains. UniProt annotates GPR34 (Q9UPC5) as a Glycoprotein with 5 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond; these lie on its extracellular N-terminus and loops. The PubMed PTM search returned 28 hits for CD244 and 8 for GPR34, but the titles shown are general signalling and pharmacology papers, not site-specific glycosylation studies. If the modeled interface involves the extracellular regions, 13 unmodeled N-glycans across the two proteins could plausibly occlude it; the reported buried surface area of 4179 A^2 with 866 contacts is very large for a 751-token complex. Whether any glycosylation site actually sits on the interface was not checked, because no fold could be run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers mentioning CD244 and GPR34 together.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for CD244 and GPR34. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction found (GPR34 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table, so that miss is uninformative for GPR34). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. UniProt gives CD48 as the ligand of CD244 and lysophosphatidylserine (a lipid) as the ligand of GPR34; neither annotation suggests a protein partner of the other's class.
Other subunits
CD48;SH2D1A;SH2D1B
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold CD244 + GPR34 and read the structural analysis for (1) interface topology on each chain, to rule out an interface that touches both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues or is simply the CD244 transmembrane helix packed against the GPR34 seven-helix bundle (a common artifact without a modeled bilayer, and one that would explain the 4179 A^2 buried area), and (2) whether any of the 8 CD244 or 5 GPR34 UniProt N-glycosylation sites lie on the interface. If a site does, re-fold with the core glycan attached. If the interface is extracellular and glycan-free, test competition with the known CD244 ligand CD48 and check co-expression in NK-cell/macrophage co-culture or spatial data before bench work. The listed other_subunits are the known CD244 ligand (CD48) and cytoplasmic adapters (SH2D1A/SAP, SH2D1B/EAT-2) from UniProt, offered as context rather than as evidence of a missing obligate subunit; CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein and CORUM was not available.

Rationale
The fold check shows the modeled A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That is topologically impossible for two single-membrane proteins in an intact membrane, so the modeled contact cannot be real. My original CONFIRMED_NOVEL verdict relied on the ipTM of 0.7, the low interface PAE and the receptor selectivity, and it noted that the membrane-side question was unchecked. This check answers that question negatively, so those scores now count as fold artifacts rather than support. The remaining evidence is also weak. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed found nothing for the pair. HPA enrichment places IL21R in T/NK cells and GPR141 in neutrophil progenitors, so there is no shared cell type. Nothing independent backs a real interaction, so I downgrade to IMPLAUSIBLE.
PTM / glycosylation

OLR1 ↔ GPR82 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.47 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows two problems that my first verdict did not account for. (1) Topology violation: the modeled OLR1-GPR82 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That cannot happen across an intact plasma membrane, so the predicted contact is physically impossible as modeled. The low ipTM (0.47) and the fact that only 1 of 3 tested partner receptors was confident also point to a nonspecific membrane-protein contact, not a real binding mode. (2) A glycosylation or lipidation site lies directly on the modeled interface. AF3 does not represent glycans or palmitoyl groups, so even the part of the contact that is topologically allowed is unreliable. OLR1 is also a C-type lectin-like receptor whose ligand binding is largely ligand- and glycan-dependent. The unresolved PTM question therefore no longer justifies INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE. Together with the absence of any supporting literature (no STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed trace, and HPA plausibility unknown), the only support left is general macrophage co-expression, which is weak. I am therefore moving to IMPLAUSIBLE at medium confidence, not high. A direct interaction through correctly oriented extracellular domains is not strictly excluded, but this fold does not support it.
PTM / glycosylation
Both proteins are glycoproteins with 2 N-glycosylation sites each. OLR1 also has lipidation sites and is a C-type lectin-like receptor. The fold check places a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled interface. AF3 cannot represent these modifications, so the contact is unreliable.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for OLR1 and GPR82 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed co-occurrence evidence for this pair, so no known-interaction credit. This is unchanged from the original triage.
Other subunits

SLITRK3 ↔ GPR83 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.61 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check found a topology violation. The modeled SLITRK3–GPR83 interface includes both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which can't happen across an intact membrane. Both proteins are single-pass (SLITRK3) or 7-TM (GPR83) membrane proteins, so one interface can't touch both sides. The very large buried area (7143 Å2, 1293 contacts) at only moderate pLDDT (61) fits a non-physiological, promiscuous contact. The marginal specificity p-value (0.0476) doesn't offset that. The novelty evidence still holds: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing (known_interaction_strength=none). But being unreported isn't enough to call it a novel interaction when the only structural support is topologically impossible. HPA plausibility is unknown, so shared brain expression is the only thing in favor. That could support an extracellular SLITRK3 LRR–GPR83 N-terminal contact, but the fold doesn't show that contact. I therefore downgrade from CONFIRMED_NOVEL to IMPLAUSIBLE as modeled, with medium confidence.
PTM / glycosylation
Both proteins are glycoproteins (SLITRK3 has 2 sites, GPR83 has 3). No site was placed on the interface, so no PTM artifact was established. The topology violation is the main concern.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for SLITRK3 and GPR83 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 papers) all returned nothing, so the pair is not known. This is a lack of a literature trace, not support for the interaction.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold using only the extracellular domains (SLITRK3 LRR ectodomain with the GPR83 N-terminus and extracellular loops), or run a membrane-aware model. Check whether the interface becomes topologically consistent. Consider adding glycans.

TNFRSF13B ↔ GPR65 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
The real AF3 fold shows the TNFRSF13B–GPR65 interface spanning both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. Both are single-membrane proteins, so one contact surface cannot lie on both sides of an intact bilayer. The ipTM of 0.57 is barely above threshold and so does not outweigh this. The fold also places a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the interface. AF3 does not model glycans, so the contact is probably not a real binding mode. The absence of STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed hits remains true (known_interaction_strength=none). It no longer supports "confirmed novel", because it is now paired with a structural impossibility. The original single-membrane co-localization argument was only hypothetical. Single-cell expression does not overlap (B/plasma cells versus mast cells), so there is no supporting evidence that these proteins would ever meet. The topology violation and the interface glycan together mean the predicted interaction is most likely a docking artifact.
PTM / glycosylation
The fold flags a glycosylation/lipidation site directly on the modeled interface. UniProt lists TNFRSF13B with 1 glycosylation site and GPR65 with 3. AF3 omits glycans, so the contact geometry is unreliable. Neither protein is a lectin, so this is a modeling caveat rather than a glycan-mediated mechanism.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for TNFRSF13B and GPR65 returned a count of 0, with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength=none). That makes it unreported, but it gives no positive support for a real interaction.
Other subunits
TNFSF13;TNFSF13B
Follow-up (not yet executed): No further follow-up is needed unless independent experimental evidence appears. If so, re-run the pair using only the extracellular domains, or only the transmembrane/cytoplasmic regions, with the glycans represented.

SEMA4C ↔ GPR37 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.6 · pTM
Rationale
I'm changing the verdict from CONFIRMED_NOVEL to IMPLAUSIBLE because both contradictions hold up. (1) HPA shows zero shared protein-level tissue expression, and neither protein is secreted. My original argument leaned on UniProt's shared "cell membrane/synapse" labels, and that is only a generic compartment match. It gives no evidence that the two are ever in the same cell or tissue. (2) The real AF3 fold puts the interface across both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues. That can't happen across an intact membrane, so the moderate ipTM of 0.6 is probably a spurious contact between a membrane-spanning segment and a domain on the other side of the membrane. The lack of any STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed trace for the pair still means it isn't already known. But novelty doesn't rescue a pair that has no route to meet and a topologically impossible modelled interface.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt carries the Glycoprotein keyword for both proteins: SEMA4C has 6 annotated glycosylation sites and 6 disulfide bonds, and GPR37 has 3 annotated glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. These sites are expected to lie on the extracellular regions. The PubMed PTM searches (5 hits for SEMA4C, 10 for GPR37) returned mainly signalling papers and nothing specific about glycosylation of either protein's interface region; the one glycosylation title for GPR37 concerns its ligand prosaposin, not GPR37 itself. Neither protein has a glycan-mediated (lectin-type) binding mechanism in its UniProt function text. The modelled interface is large (buried surface area 2392.0 Å2, 411 contacts), so overlap with a glycan site on the SEMA4C ectodomain or the GPR37 N-terminus is possible, but it could not be checked because no fold was available in this run. This is an unresolved caveat, not evidence of an artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for SEMA4C and GPR37 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all found nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength=none), so it is not already known. That only establishes novelty and does not support the interaction.
Other subunits
PLXNB2
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is still of interest, re-model only the extracellular domains of SEMA4C and GPR37, and check for co-expression in single-cell data, for example in oligodendrocyte-lineage cells.

CALCB ↔ MAS1L CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
CALCB (CGRP2, a secreted peptide hormone) and MAS1L (an orphan Mas-related GPCR at the plasma membrane) are a compartment-plausible ligand-receptor pair with no prior record: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed returned 0 co-mentions. The supplied screen data show the fold is selective for this receptor (confident against 1 of 13 receptors tested, p_twoway=0.028535, interface PAE 3.16 A, no clash at min separation 2.29 A). Confidence is low because ipTM is 0.48, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, interface pLDDT is only 61.2, and the 505-token complex with 5512 A^2 buried surface implies the full CALCB precursor was folded rather than the mature 37-residue amidated peptide, so part of the interface may involve propeptide that does not exist on the circulating hormone.
PTM / glycosylation
MAS1L (UniProt P35410) carries the Glycoprotein keyword and 2 annotated glycosylation sites. Their positions were not returned; if they lie on the extracellular N-terminus or loops they could sit at or near a peptide-binding surface, and this could not be checked without a fold. The PubMed PTM search for MAS1L returned 0 hits. CALCB (UniProt P10092) has no glycosylation; it has 1 disulfide bond and 1 modified residue, and the keywords indicate C-terminal amidation and cleavage at paired basic residues. The mature peptide is therefore a processed, amidated fragment that the bare-sequence precursor fold does not represent. The 3 PubMed PTM hits for CALCB were not relevant to PTMs at this interface. This is a caveat, not enough on its own for an artifact call.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CALCB and MAS1L returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CALCB-MAS1L. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and MAS1L is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The only documented CALCB receptor per UniProt is the CALCRL-RAMP1 complex. No hit in any source supports genuine novelty.
Other subunits
RAMP1
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold could be run in this triage. Recommended next steps: (1) Re-fold MAS1L with only the mature CGRP2 peptide (37 residues, Cys2-Cys7 disulfide, C-terminal amide) rather than the full precursor, and check that chain-pair ipTM reaches at least 0.5 and that the peptide contacts the extracellular/transmembrane pocket rather than cytoplasmic residues. (2) Check whether the 2 MAS1L glycosylation sites sit on the modeled interface, and if so re-fold with the core N-glycan attached. (3) Consider a fold including RAMP1, since CGRP's canonical receptor CALCRL needs a RAMP; whether MAS1L associates with any RAMP is unknown, so this is speculative. CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein. (4) Wet-lab test: CGRP2 stimulation of MAS1L-expressing cells with a beta-arrestin recruitment or Ca2+/cAMP readout, using CALCRL-RAMP1 as the positive control.
Validation strategy: Stimulation assay (Mature amidated CALCB(1-37) peptide vs. precursor/propeptide-containing construct, with and without RAMP1 co-expression)
Why this method
CALCB is a secreted peptide hormone and MAS1L is an orphan plasma-membrane GPCR, so the pair is paracrine/endocrine and the two proteins need not be in the same cell. A functional readout (cAMP, Gq/Ca2+ or β-arrestin) tests whether the ligand activates the receptor. That is more informative than co-localization, and it also shows whether the mature peptide works or only the precursor-like fold does.
Target tissue(s)
thyroid gland, brain, cervix
Why these tissues (HPA)
There is no real HPA overlap. CALCB RNA is highest in thyroid gland (19.3 nTPM) and brain (8.7 nTPM), and it is secreted to blood. MAS1L RNA is only detected in cervix (2.9 nTPM, low). The ligand source (thyroid/brain) and the receptor tissue (cervix) are therefore separate, which fits an endocrine model. The assay uses recombinant peptide on receptor-expressing cells, so it does not need native co-expression.
Cell type(s)
HEK293 cells transfected with MAS1L (plus or minus RAMP1), cervical epithelial/stromal cells (endogenous MAS1L, if detectable), thyroid C-cells (CALCB source, for co-culture)
Protocol notes
1) Express MAS1L (N-terminal epitope-tagged, with surface expression confirmed by anti-tag flow cytometry or ELISA) in HEK293 or CHO cells, with and without RAMP1. Add synthetic mature amidated CALCB(1-37) in a dose-response (1 pM–10 µM). Read out cAMP (GloSensor/HTRF), Ca2+ flux (Fluo-4) and β-arrestin recruitment (PathHunter/BRET), because the G-protein coupling of MAS1L is unknown. 2) Compare mature amidated CALCB, non-amidated CALCB, and a propeptide-containing precursor or conditioned medium from CALCB-transfected cells, to test whether the predicted interface depends on propeptide that is absent from the circulating hormone. 3) Run a binding confirmation with labelled CALCB (fluorescent or biotinylated peptide) on MAS1L-expressing versus mock cells, with competition by unlabelled peptide. 4) If a response is seen, check RAMP1 dependence and test a CGRP receptor antagonist (e.g. olcegepant/CGRP8-37) to separate any effect from CALCRL/RAMP1.
Controls
Positive: CALCB on CALCRL/RAMP1 cells (a known CGRP receptor response, cAMP); a known MAS1L-family agonist, such as BAM8-22 on MRGPRX1 or Ang-(1-7) on MAS1, as a GPCR assay positive. Negative: mock-transfected parental cells; an unrelated Mas-related GPCR (e.g. MRGPRX2) with CALCB; scrambled or irrelevant peptide; vehicle; MAS1L-expressing cells given an unrelated peptide hormone.
Caveats
Confidence is low: ipTM is 0.48, interface pLDDT is 61.2, and the model was probably built from the full CALCB precursor (505 tokens, 5512 Å² buried surface), so the interface may involve propeptide that the mature 37-residue amidated hormone does not have. MAS1L RNA in HPA is very low (cervix 2.9 nTPM) and there is no protein or single-cell data, so no native cell model is guaranteed and overexpression will be needed. MAS1L is an orphan receptor with unknown coupling, so a negative result in one readout does not rule out activation. Possible CALCRL/RAMP1 cross-talk in the host cells could confound the readout. The RAMP1 role is unclear, since CALCB-RAMP1 normally acts through CALCRL.
