CDH2 ↔ GPR75 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.31 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the predicted A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That is topologically impossible across an intact membrane, so the pose can't be a real cis contact. My earlier INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE verdict didn't account for this. The other evidence doesn't rescue the pose. ipTM is 0.31 and interface pLDDT is 58.1. The 1.6 A minimum separation with 1414 contacts over 7452 A^2 looks like an over-packed, clashing pose. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all found nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength = none). The only support is that both proteins sit at the plasma membrane. HPA plausibility is unknown, so there is no shared-expression data to offset the topology problem. CDH2 is a homotypic adhesion molecule and GPR75 is a CCL5/20-HETE-responsive GPCR. Because the one modeled interface is topologically invalid and nothing independent supports the pair, I'm classing it as implausible. The glycosylation concern on CDH2 (7 sites) and GPR75 (3 sites) is secondary, since the pose is already rejected on topology.
PTM / glycosylation
CDH2 has 7 glycosylation sites and GPR75 has 3. They were not checked against the interface residues. This is moot because the pose already fails the topology check.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for CDH2 and GPR75 together.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: not found. CellPhoneDB: no match. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. Known-interaction strength is none.
Other subunits
CTNNB1;CTNND1;CCL5
Follow-up (not yet executed): Only revisit this if independent experimental evidence appears, such as co-IP or proximity ligation in a cell type that expresses both. Any re-fold should be restricted to the extracellular domains, or to the topologically compatible domains only.

ICOSLG ↔ GPR34 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.52 · pTM
Rationale
ICOSLG-GPR34 is unreported in every source checked (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), and the two proteins could plausibly meet at the plasma membrane of myeloid cells. However, both are glycoproteins with 5 annotated glycosylation sites each (UniProt O75144 and Q9UPC5), and AF3 folded them as bare sequence. With a marginal ipTM of 0.52 and a very large modeled interface (2816 A^2 buried, 648 contacts), the interface plausibly overlaps glycan-bearing extracellular surface. I could not verify whether any glycosylation site actually sits on the interface, because no fold or per-residue tool was available in this run, so this is a caveat-driven call, not a demonstrated artifact. GPR34's documented ligand is also a lipid (lysophosphatidylserine), not a protein.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates ICOSLG (O75144) with the Glycoprotein keyword, 5 glycosylation sites and 2 disulfide bonds. GPR34 (Q9UPC5) also carries the Glycoprotein keyword, with 5 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. For a single-pass type I protein and a GPCR these sites are expected to lie on the extracellular side, but the tools did not return their positions. The PubMed PTM searches were uninformative: 1 hit for ICOSLG (a CD37 lymphoma paper) and 8 for GPR34 (ligand, antagonist and lymphoma-mutation papers), none specifically about glycosylation of an interface. Ten unmodeled glycosylation sites across the two proteins, against a 2816 A^2 interface, make steric overlap plausible but unconfirmed.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for ICOSLG and GPR34 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for ICOSLG-GPR34. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR34 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is novel with respect to these sources. ICOSLG's documented partner is ICOS; GPR34's documented ligand is lysophosphatidylserine.
Other subunits
ICOS
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Re-fold ICOSLG + GPR34 with AF3 and read the interface topology and PTM-at-interface report. This checks whether any of the 5 + 5 glycosylation sites lie on the contact surface, and whether the interface is ectodomain-to-extracellular-loop or merely packing of the ICOSLG transmembrane helix against the GPR34 helical bundle. The latter is a common bilayer-free artifact and would also explain the large buried area. 2) If a glycosylation site is on the interface, re-fold with the core N-glycans attached. 3) Check that the modeled interface does not occupy the ICOS-binding face of the ICOSLG IgV domain, or fold with ICOS as a competitor. 4) If the interface survives, test at the bench with glycosylated proteins: co-immunoprecipitation or a proximity assay in macrophage or microglia-like cells, or ICOSLG-Fc binding to GPR34-expressing cells.

MST1 ↔ GPR37 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.52 · pTM
Rationale
No prior record of this pair exists in any source checked: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pairing is compartment-plausible as a secreted ligand and membrane receptor: HPA classes MST1 (macrophage stimulating 1) as a predicted secreted protein, "Secreted to blood", and GPR37 is a G protein-coupled receptor that UniProt (O15354) places at the cell membrane. Confidence is low because the fold is borderline (ipTM 0.52, interface pLDDT 70.9, p_twoway 0.050324), the glycosylation check is incomplete, and the identity of the folded MST1 sequence needs confirming.
PTM / glycosylation
GPR37 (UniProt O15354) carries the Glycoprotein keyword and 3 annotated glycosylation sites, plus 1 disulfide bond. For a secreted ligand the contact would have to be on the extracellular side, so these glycans are plausibly relevant, but the site positions relative to the modeled interface are unknown; this is an unresolved caveat, not a demonstrated artifact. The PubMed PTM search for GPR37 returned 10 papers, none of the 5 listed being specifically about GPR37's own glycosylation. The MST1 side of the check is not usable: uniprot_annotation returned Q13043 (STK4, serine/threonine-protein kinase 4), an intracellular Hippo-pathway kinase that shares the alias "MST1", and the 406 PubMed PTM hits are likewise dominated by the Hippo kinase. No tool result was therefore obtained on glycosylation of the secreted macrophage stimulating 1 protein. From background knowledge, not from a tool, that protein is a glycosylated plasminogen-like protein secreted as a single-chain precursor that is proteolytically activated.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for MST1 and GPR37 returned a count of 0, with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for MST1 and GPR37 (found: false). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction (found: false, no matches). PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. UniProt lists prosaposin, osteocalcin and neuroprotectin D1 as the GPR37 ligands; MST1 is not among them. No hit in any of the three sources is fairly strong evidence that the pair is unreported.
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1. Confirm which sequence was folded as "MST1": macrophage stimulating 1 (the HGNC gene, secreted) or STK4 (Q13043, alias MST1, an intracellular kinase). If it was STK4, this triage does not apply and the pair must be re-assessed as an intracellular kinase against a GPCR, plausible only on the cytoplasmic side. 2. Inspect the supplied model: the minimum separation of 1.98 Å is short for 516 contacts and should be checked for clashes, and the interface should be checked for sitting on the GPR37 extracellular N-terminal domain and loops, not on cytoplasmic residues. 3. Fold recommended but not available in this run: the pair with structural analysis to see whether any of the 3 GPR37 glycosylation sites (or sites on macrophage stimulating 1) fall on the interface, then a glycan-attached re-fold if they do. 4. The supplied counter-screen (1 of 3 receptors confident at interface PAE <= 3.75 Å) is mildly supportive of specificity; extend it to include MST1R, which from background knowledge is the established receptor for this ligand, as a positive control. 5. Wet-lab: binding of the mature, activated protein to GPR37-expressing cells, with an ERK phosphorylation or receptor internalization readout.
Validation strategy: Stimulation assay
Why this method
MST1 is a secreted hepatic protein (secreted to blood) and GPR37 is a GPCR, so this is an endocrine ligand-receptor pair. The two proteins are not expected in the same cell. A functional readout in receptor-expressing cells tests the ligand-receptor hypothesis better than co-localization.
Target tissue(s)
liver, brain, cerebral cortex, retina
Why these tissues (HPA)
There is no real tissue overlap. MST1 RNA is liver-specific (864 nTPM, hepatocytes 369 nCPM). GPR37 is found in brain (67 nTPM), retina (36 nTPM) and cerebral cortex at the protein level. Müller glia (809 nCPM) and oligodendrocytes (388 nCPM) express it most. Circulating MST1 could reach the CNS or retina only across the blood-brain or blood-retina barrier, so the assay uses recombinant ligand on receptor-bearing cells, or on cells engineered to express GPR37.
Cell type(s)
Hepatocytes (MST1 source), Oligodendrocytes (GPR37 side), Müller glia (GPR37 side)
Protocol notes
1) Use HEK293 cells, or a glial line such as an oligodendrocyte precursor line, stably expressing GPR37. Use empty-vector or GPR37-knockout cells as the control. Add a surface tag to GPR37 and check surface localization, because HPA lists it at the nuclear membrane and it is prone to ER retention. 2) Stimulate with recombinant human MST1 (active, preferably the two-chain form) at 1 nM to 1 µM for 5 to 60 min. Read out Gi/o and Gq signalling: cAMP (GloSensor), Ca2+ flux, ERK1/2 phosphorylation, and β-arrestin recruitment (BRET or PathHunter). 3) Test binding directly with a cell-surface binding assay, for example biotinylated or Fc-tagged MST1 on GPR37 versus control cells with flow cytometry. Confirm that the binding is competed by untagged MST1. 4) Optionally co-culture with MST1-secreting hepatocytes (HepG2) or use conditioned medium from them, and include an anti-MST1 neutralizing antibody.
Controls
Positive controls: prosaposin or its peptide TX14(A), a reported GPR37 ligand, and MST1 acting on its known receptor RON (MST1R) as a ligand-activity control, for example in RON-expressing cells. Negative controls: GPR37-null parental cells, heat-denatured MST1, an unrelated serine protease-like protein, and a GPR37L1-expressing line to test specificity. Also include a RON inhibitor or RON-negative cells, so that any signal can be attributed to GPR37 rather than endogenous RON.
Caveats
Confidence is low: ipTM is 0.52, interface pLDDT is 70.9 and p_twoway is 0.05. The glycosylation check is incomplete, and the identity of the folded MST1 sequence (pro-form versus mature α/β chains) needs confirming. HPA lists GPR37 at the nuclear membrane, and GPR37 is often misfolded or ER-retained when overexpressed, so surface expression must be checked. MST1 acts through RON, which could confound the signalling readouts. HPA also predicts MST1 as intracellular and vesicular, but it is mainly circulating plasma protein. Tissue distribution makes a physiological endocrine interaction hard to justify, because MST1 would have to cross the blood-brain barrier. A negative result in an overexpression system does not rule out a requirement for a co-factor.

EPHA5 ↔ GPR158 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.38 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check that was unavailable at the original triage has now run and found a topology violation. The modeled EPHA5–GPR158 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which cannot happen across an intact membrane. That fits the original suspicion that the very large interface (7846 A^2, 1636 contacts) reflects bilayer-free transmembrane packing, not a real contact. Together with the weak confidence (ipTM 0.38, interface pLDDT 59.4) and no STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed support, the structural hit does not stand as a genuine interaction. The brain co-expression and synaptic co-localization (HPA plausibility: plausible) mean the pair could still meet in principle, so confidence is medium and not high. A real interaction, if one exists, would need a different, topologically valid interface, which this fold does not show.
PTM / glycosylation
Both proteins are glycoproteins (EPHA5 has 6 annotated sites, GPR158 has 5), but neither binds through a glycan-mediated mechanism. The glycosylation concern is secondary to the topology violation.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for EPHA5 and GPR158 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all found nothing for the pair (known_interaction_strength=none). GPR158 is absent from the CellPhoneDB gene table, so that miss is uninformative for GPR158.
Other subunits
EFNA5;RGS7;GNB5;GPC4
Follow-up (not yet executed): If the pair is still of interest, test the extracellular domains of the two proteins alone, or the transmembrane-restricted region with topology-aware constraints. Alternatively, validate by co-immunoprecipitation in neurons.

FGFR3 ↔ GPR21 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.67 · pTM
Rationale
The fold-based topology check changes my verdict. The AF3 model's FGFR3-GPR21 interface (about 4107 Å², 891 contacts) includes both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. Two intact single-pass and 7TM membrane proteins cannot form that contact, because it would have to cross the membrane. My earlier INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE verdict did not account for this. The giant interface looks like an artifact of folding two membrane proteins as bare sequences with no bilayer. Nothing else offsets this. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 co-mentions) give no known interaction, so there is no prior evidence to outweigh the topology problem. HPA shows no protein-level or subcellular data for GPR21, and its RNA (blood vessel only) does not overlap with FGFR3-enriched tissues. The glycosylation caveat is secondary, since AF3 omits glycans. The pair stays novel in the literature, but the modeled interface is not a credible physical contact.
PTM / glycosylation
Both are glycoproteins (FGFR3 6 sites, GPR21 2). This is secondary, because the topology violation already makes the modeled interface non-physical.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for FGFR3 and GPR21 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed (0 papers) record for this pair, so no known interaction. Known_interaction_strength is none.
Other subunits
FGF1;FGF2
Follow-up (not yet executed): If the pair is of interest, test only extracellular-domain constructs of each protein, or use a membrane-aware method. Experimental co-expression and co-IP in a shared cell type would also be needed.

CCK ↔ GPR6 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
The AF3 ipTM of 0.48 is below the standard 0.5 AF-Multimer/AF3 threshold for "predicting a real interaction", and the interface pLDDT of 63.9 is only moderate, so the structural evidence does not support the pair on its own. More importantly, UniProt (P06307) shows the bioactive cholecystokinin peptide depends on PTMs AF3 cannot represent — keywords "Sulfation", "Amidation", "Glycoprotein", "Proteoglycan", "Cleavage on pair of basic residues" (4 Modified residue features, 1 Glycosylation site); the C-terminal amide and the sulfated Tyr are precisely what confer CCK receptor binding, and AF3 modelled a bare, uncleaved, unmodified sequence. GPR6 (P46095) is additionally annotated as activated by lipid-derived agonists (oleic acid, sphingosine 1-phosphate) and is constitutively active, i.e. its documented mechanism is not peptide-hormone binding.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt P06307 (CCK) keywords include Sulfation, Amidation, Glycoprotein and Proteoglycan, with 4 Modified residue and 1 Glycosylation feature; CCK's receptor-binding activity classically requires the sulfated tyrosine and the C-terminal amide, neither of which AF3 modelled, and the mature peptide is also generated by proprotein cleavage that AF3 did not apply. UniProt P46095 (GPR6) carries 3 Glycosylation sites and 1 Lipidation (palmitate) site on a 7TM receptor, i.e. extracellular N-glycans near the ligand-entry face that AF3 also omits. pubmed_ptm_glycosylation was uninformative for both (3242 largely off-target hits for CCK, 8 generic GPCR-signalling papers for GPR6).
PubMed co-occurrence
1 PubMed hit (PMID 18588537), an amygdala transcriptional-plasticity study listing both genes among regulated transcripts; unrelated to a physical interaction.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned found=false for CCK–GPR6 (no channel evidence at all). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence returned 1 hit (PMID 18588537, "Mu-opioid receptor activation induces transcriptional plasticity in the central extended amygdala"), which is a transcriptomic co-mention of two genes in the same brain region, not a report of binding. So the pair is genuinely unreported as an interaction.
Follow-up (not yet executed): A fold could not be run in this session. Recommended: re-run AF3 with the true mature CCK-8/CCK-33 fragment rather than the full preprohormone, with sulfo-Tyr and the C-terminal amide represented, and with GPR6's N-glycans modelled, and check whether a transmembrane-bundle orthosteric interface appears and whether a glycosylation site sits on the modeled contact surface. In parallel, a counter-screen folding the same CCK peptide against CCKAR/CCKBR (true receptors) and 2-3 unrelated class-A GPCRs would show whether any GPR6 signal is specific. Wet-lab: cAMP/GloSensor assay on GPR6-expressing cells with sulfated CCK-8, given GPR6's high constitutive Gs activity.

GHRL ↔ GPR32 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
The AF3 ipTM for this pair is 0.49, below the standard AF-Multimer/AF3 0.5 convention for "a real interaction is being predicted", and the interface pLDDT is only 67.2 — so the model is not actually supporting the pair despite the large buried area (3702 A^2) and low interface PAE. Biologically the pairing is also weak: UniProt (Q9UBU3) states that octanoylation of ghrelin-28 is ESSENTIAL for receptor activation (3 Lipidation features), a modification AF3 does not represent at all, while GPR32 (O75388) is a lipid-mediator receptor whose characterized high-affinity ligand is resolvin D1, a chemically unrelated oxylipin rather than a peptide hormone. Combined with zero evidence in STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed, this looks like a sub-threshold peptide-in-GPCR-pocket pose rather than a real ligand-receptor pair.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt GHRL (Q9UBU3) carries keyword "Lipoprotein" and 3 Lipidation features; the FUNCTION text explicitly says octanoylation of ghrelin-28 is essential for activation of its receptor GHSR — AF3 folds bare sequence and cannot represent this acyl chain, which is the key pharmacophore of ghrelin. UniProt GPR32 (O75388) is a Glycoprotein with 2 N-glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond on an extracellular-facing GPCR surface; a glycan there could occlude part of the modeled contact surface. PubMed PTM searches were uninformative (GHRL 5 hits, none about ghrelin acylation; GPR32 7 hits, all resolvin D1 pharmacology papers).
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned count=0 with no hits for GHRL and GPR32.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for GHRL-GPR32 (found=false). CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (found=false, no matches), and GPR32 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers mentioning both genes. So there is no prior literature or database claim for this pair — it would be genuinely novel if it were real, but nothing independently supports it.
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is pursued anyway, the fold I could not run here (fold_complex is disabled in this run) would be GHRL + GPR32 re-run with the octanoyl group attached at Ser3 of the mature ghrelin peptide, plus the two UniProt N-glycans on GPR32, to see whether the interface survives acylation/glycosylation and whether contacts fall inside the transmembrane bundle (orthosteric) rather than only on extracellular loops. Experimentally, a GPR32 calcium/cAMP or beta-arrestin assay with acyl-ghrelin versus des-acyl ghrelin, counter-screened against GHSR and an unrelated GPCR, would settle it; but given ipTM 0.49 is below threshold and GPR32's endogenous ligand is a lipid mediator, this is low priority for bench time.

CFC1 ↔ GPR148 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.53 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows that the A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues of GPR148. That can't happen across an intact membrane, and CFC1 is extracellular and GPI-anchored, so it can only reach the extracellular face. The modelled contact therefore rests partly on residues CFC1 could never touch. The ipTM of 0.53 and interface pLDDT of 61.5 were already borderline, and the topology violation makes them look like an artifact of modelling the receptor without its membrane. No other evidence offsets this. There is no STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed support (known_interaction_strength = none). GPR148 RNA is not detected in any HPA tissue, so there is no shared expression to show the two would meet. HPA compartment compatibility (secreted/membrane) only makes the pair plausible in principle. I am changing the verdict from CONFIRMED_NOVEL to IMPLAUSIBLE. A bare-sequence AF3 fold can't be taken as a real interaction when the interface is topologically impossible. A genuine extracellular-only contact remains possible but is unsupported.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists CFC1 (P0CG37) as a Glycoprotein and Lipoprotein with 1 Glycosylation site, 1 Lipidation site (the GPI anchor) and 3 Disulfide bonds. GPR148 (Q8TDV2) is a Glycoprotein with 1 Glycosylation site. The PubMed PTM search returned 4 hits for CFC1, none of which specifically addresses CFC1 glycosylation at a binding surface, and 0 hits for GPR148. Both glycosylation sites are expected on extracellular regions, which is where this interface would have to be, so a glycan could sterically affect the modelled contact; whether either site lies on the interface is unknown, so this is recorded as an unresolved caveat and not as evidence of an artifact. The GPI anchor at the CFC1 C-terminus is not modelled by AF3 and constrains how CFC1 can be oriented relative to the membrane.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CFC1 and GPR148 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing (known_interaction_strength = none). There is no prior support for the pair, and none arises from this reconsideration.
Other subunits
ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C;NODAL
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-run AF3 with only the GPR148 extracellular N-terminus and loops against CFC1, or add the co-receptor and ligand partners (e.g., NODAL, TDGF1/ACVR2B). Only keep the pair if the interface sits entirely on the extracellular face.

CLSTN1 ↔ GPR22 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check found a topology violation. The modeled CLSTN1–GPR22 interface includes both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, and that can't happen across an intact membrane. My original verdict called the pair "plausible but unverified" and didn't address this. The interface is very large (5160 Å², 1015 contacts), and ipTM is only 0.49 with interface pLDDT of 60. That pattern fits a spurious bare-sequence docking of two membrane proteins, not a real contact. The novelty evidence still holds: STRING and CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. But "no prior record" is not support for the interaction. The shared brain-neuron expression and plasma-membrane localization only show the proteins could meet. They don't rescue a geometrically impossible pose. A real interaction might exist through a topologically valid contact, such as extracellular-only or transmembrane-only. This model doesn't show one, so I'm downgrading from CONFIRMED_NOVEL.
PTM / glycosylation
Both proteins are annotated as glycoproteins (CLSTN1 has 3 sites, GPR22 has 2). I couldn't place these sites relative to the interface. This is secondary to the topology violation.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CLSTN1 and GPR22 returned a count of 0, with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 papers) all returned nothing for this pair, so known_interaction_strength is none. This supports novelty only. It doesn't validate the modeled interface.
Other subunits
NRXN1;KLC1;APBA2;APP

CFC1 ↔ GPR33 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.58 · pTM
Rationale
The fold analysis shows the CFC1–GPR33 interface spanning both cytoplasmic and extracellular GPR33 residues. That is topologically impossible across an intact membrane, since a secreted or GPI-anchored extracellular CFC1 can only reach the extracellular face of GPR33. This means the borderline AF3 contact (ipTM 0.58, interface pLDDT 64.1) is probably an artifact of modelling the two proteins as bare sequences with no membrane context. The fold therefore cannot count as support for the pair, and I am no longer treating the case as merely under-evidenced. The remaining evidence also offers no rescue: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all show no known interaction, GPR33 RNA is not detected in HPA at tissue or single-cell level, and CFC1 also folds confidently against 3 of the 14 receptors it was tested with, which suggests non-specific confidence. The topological plausibility of a ligand meeting a GPCR still holds in principle, but the specific modelled contact violates membrane topology, so I downgrade to IMPLAUSIBLE. Confidence is medium because the extracellular-only part of the interface was not assessed separately.
PTM / glycosylation
Both proteins are glycoproteins (GPR33 has 4 glycosylation sites, CFC1 has 1 plus a GPI anchor), and AF3 did not model these. This is secondary to the topology violation, which is the deciding concern.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned a count of 0 with no hits for CFC1 and GPR33.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association, CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction (GPR33 is absent from its gene table), and PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. known_interaction_strength=none, so the pair is novel but has no supporting literature.
Other subunits
ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C;NODAL
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is still of interest, re-fold CFC1 against only the extracellular N-terminus and loops of GPR33 and check that the interface stays on the extracellular face. Confirm GPR33 expression in a relevant cell type first.

FLT1 ↔ GPR4 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.6 · pTM
Rationale
No database records this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, one incidental PubMed co-mention), and both are plasma-membrane receptors with a vascular endothelial role, so a lateral in-membrane contact is topologically possible. However, UniProt annotates both as glycoproteins (FLT1 P17948: 13 glycosylation sites, on a receptor whose glycosylated portion is extracellular; GPR4 P46093: 2 sites), and AF3 folded bare sequence with only moderate confidence (ipTM 0.6, interface pLDDT 63.7). I could not determine which residues form the modeled 3506 A^2 interface, so the glycan-occlusion concern is plausible but unverified; this is a low-confidence call that could become CONFIRMED_NOVEL if the interface proves to be transmembrane or cytoplasmic.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates FLT1 (P17948) with the Glycoprotein keyword and 13 glycosylation sites, plus 6 disulfide bonds and 8 modified residues; it is also a Phosphoprotein. UniProt annotates GPR4 (P46093) with the Glycoprotein keyword and 2 glycosylation sites. The PubMed PTM searches returned 208 hits for FLT1 and 14 for GPR4, but the top titles were not specific to glycosylation of either protein, so UniProt is the informative source here. Any extracellular portion of the modeled interface (3506 A^2 buried, 648 contacts) would plausibly be affected by glycans that AF3 did not model. Whether a glycosylation site actually lies on the interface was not checked because no fold or per-residue analysis was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence found 1 paper (PMID 34870494, Int J Biol Markers 2022, "System analysis of VEGFA in renal cell carcinoma: the expression, prognosis, gene regulation network and regulation targets"). Both genes appear there in a VEGFA-related network analysis; it does not show that FLT1 and GPR4 physically interact.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for FLT1-GPR4. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR4 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 1 hit (PMID 34870494, a system analysis of the VEGFA gene regulation network in renal cell carcinoma), which is a bioinformatic co-mention and not a report of direct binding. The pair is effectively unreported.
Other subunits
KDR
Follow-up (not yet executed): Map the interface residues of the existing AF3 model onto UniProt topology for P17948 and P46093. First, confirm the interface does not touch both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues at once, which would be physically impossible. Second, check whether any of the 13 FLT1 or 2 GPR4 glycosylation sites lie on the interface; if so, re-fold with core N-glycans attached at those sites. If the interface is purely transmembrane or cytoplasmic and glycan-free, upgrade to CONFIRMED_NOVEL and test by co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in endothelial cells (e.g. HUVEC) under acidic versus neutral pH. Also consider re-folding with KDR, the CellPhoneDB-curated FLT1 heterodimer partner, and extending the specificity counter-screen: the supplied data show the partner was confident against 1 of 2 receptors tested, which is too small a panel to establish specificity.

SST ↔ MRGPRX2 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.26 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the A-B interface touching both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues of MRGPRX2. That is impossible across an intact membrane, because a secreted SST cannot reach the intracellular face. The pose is therefore not a physically valid binding mode, and this adds to the weak ipTM (0.26) and interface pLDDT (64.1). The large buried area and low interface PAE are probably artifacts of that non-physical pose, so they don't support the pair. My earlier INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE verdict did not account for this. The pair still has no database support: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all came back empty (known_interaction_strength=none). HPA plausibility is fine, since SST is secreted and MRGPRX2 is a surface receptor. The paralog cortistatin-14 is a documented MRGPRX2 agonist, so a real somatostatin interaction remains conceivable. However, this fold gives no support for it, and the topology violation rules out the modeled pose. I therefore classify this specific AF3 prediction as implausible, not as a confirmed or novel interaction. Medium confidence, because the biology could still hold, and a re-fold with mature SST-14 against the extracellular or transmembrane pocket is needed to test it.
PTM / glycosylation
Neither protein carries a glycosylation or lipidation concern that AF3 would miss: SST's ptm_topology_feature_counts are 1 Modified residue and 1 Disulfide bond (zero Glycosylation), with keywords "Amidation", "Cleavage on pair of basic residues", "Disulfide bond"; MRGPRX2 has 2 Disulfide bonds and zero Glycosylation annotated. The PubMed PTM searches (126 hits for SST, 41 for MRGPRX2) returned titles about neuronal subtypes, gastric cancer and mast-cell pseudo-allergy — unrelated to modification of a contact surface. One real caveat that is NOT a glycan issue: mature somatostatin is produced by proprotein cleavage on pairs of basic residues and is C-terminally amidated, so the bioactive species is SST-14/SST-28, not the full preprosomatostatin sequence AF3 was presumably given; folding the wrong chain length could by itself explain the poor ipTM.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no record. CellPhoneDB: no match, and MRGPRX2 is absent from its gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 hits. known_interaction_strength=none. The only related evidence is UniProt listing cortistatin-14, a paralog of SST, as an MRGPRX2 agonist.
Other subunits
GNAQ;GNAI1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold mature SST-14 (and SST-28) with MRGPRX2 and check that the interface is limited to the extracellular or transmembrane-pocket side. Alternatively, test cortistatin-14 as a positive control.

SPX ↔ MRGPRX2 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.39 · pTM
Rationale
The flagged topology_violation matters, and my first verdict should have addressed it. In the AF3 fold, the SPX contacts span both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues of MRGPRX2. SPX is a secreted peptide and can only reach the extracellular face of an intact membrane receptor, so a pose that also touches the cytoplasmic side is physically impossible. That makes the modeled interface a non-physiological artifact. The fold's other weaknesses point the same way: ipTM is 0.39, interface pLDDT is 63.5, and the large buried area is expected for a flexible peptide draped over a receptor. The pair is also unsupported elsewhere: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all return nothing (known_interaction_strength=none), and SPX's documented receptors are GALR2 and GALR3. The HPA check is favourable (secreted peptide and mast-cell membrane GPCR, hpa_plausibility=plausible), so the two proteins could meet in principle. But that only shows a meeting is possible. It does not offset a fold that is topologically impossible and low-confidence, so I now classify the pair as IMPLAUSIBLE instead of INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE. The C-terminal amidation of SPX is a secondary caveat that AF3 does not model.
PTM / glycosylation
SPX carries a C-terminal amidation that AF3 does not represent. This is a secondary caveat, not the basis for the verdict. MRGPRX2 has no annotated glycosylation.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned count = 0 with no hits for SPX and MRGPRX2.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing for SPX-MRGPRX2 (known_interaction_strength=none). SPX's published receptors are GALR2 and GALR3.
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is still of interest, re-fold SPX against only the extracellular domain and transmembrane ligand-binding pocket of MRGPRX2, or test it experimentally, for example with a mast-cell degranulation or calcium assay using amidated SPX.

CD226 ↔ GPR141 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.64 · pTM
Rationale
The flagged topology violation is a real problem. The AF3 fold's A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues of intact transmembrane proteins. Two single-pass or multi-pass membrane proteins in one membrane cannot make that contact, because the two sides are separated by the bilayer. So the one confident fold (ipTM 0.64, CD226 against 1 of 13 receptors) is geometrically unphysical and cannot support a specific interaction. The original CONFIRMED_NOVEL verdict rested on that fold plus the lack of any literature record. Having no literature trace (STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all empty, known_interaction_strength=none) shows only that the pair is unreported. It is not positive evidence of an interaction. The HPA plausibility check is 'unknown' because GPR141 has no HPA localisation, so the bone marrow overlap is weak and cannot offset the topology problem. CD226's known ligands are PVR and NECTIN2, and GPR141 is an orphan receptor with no documented link to it. I downgrade the verdict to IMPLAUSIBLE at medium confidence rather than high. A trans (cell-to-cell) ectodomain contact has not been excluded, but the modeled contact does not show one.
PTM / glycosylation
CD226 has 8 glycosylation sites and GPR141 has 2. Neither has a glycan-mediated binding mechanism in its function text. This is secondary to the topology violation.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CD226 and GPR141 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength=none). This means the pair is unreported, not that it interacts.
Follow-up (not yet executed): If this pair is still of interest, re-fold only the extracellular domains (CD226 ectodomain with the GPR141 N-terminus and extracellular loops) to test for a trans interaction. Otherwise drop the candidate.

LRP8 ↔ GPR3 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the modeled A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That cannot happen across an intact membrane, because LRP8 and GPR3 are both single-membrane proteins and their cytoplasmic and extracellular faces are separated. My original rationale missed this, and it matters because the interface is very large (3870 A^2, 707 contacts) and its pLDDT is low (65.7). The 1.94 A minimum separation and the ipTM of 0.48 (below 0.5) point the same way: the pose looks like an unphysical in-silico docking and not a real contact. The earlier points in favour (shared membrane localization, plausible HPA co-expression, a favourable breadth control, an interface PAE of 3.52 A) do not fix a topologically impossible pose. The absence of any STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed trace (known_interaction_strength=none) only shows the pair is unreported, not that it is real. The unresolved glycosylation caveat is now secondary. I downgrade from CONFIRMED_NOVEL to IMPLAUSIBLE for this modeled interface. A real interaction would need a topologically valid, same-side contact, for example between the two extracellular regions or the two cytoplasmic tails, and would need re-modeling to test.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates LRP8 as a Glycoprotein with 6 glycosylation sites and 27 disulfide bonds in its extracellular LDL-receptor and EGF-like repeat region, and GPR3 as a Glycoprotein and palmitoylated Lipoprotein with 1 glycosylation site, 1 lipidation site and 3 modified residues. The PubMed PTM search returned 12 hits for LRP8 and 6 for GPR3, but the titles returned are not specifically about glycosylation at a binding surface (the GPR3 hits include GRK2/beta-arrestin-2 regulation of surface localization). The modeled interface is large (buried surface area 3870 A^2, 707 contacts), so it could plausibly involve the glycosylated LRP8 ectodomain, but whether any glycosylation site actually sits on the interface was not determined. This is an unresolved caveat, not a demonstrated artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for LRP8 and GPR3 returned a count of 0, with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
No STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed co-occurrence trace (strength none), so the pair is unreported. This does not rescue a topologically impossible pose.
Other subunits
RELN;DAB1;APOE
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-model using only the ectodomain of LRP8 against the extracellular loops and N-terminus of GPR3, or the two cytoplasmic regions, so the interface stays on one side of the membrane. Check whether any glycosylation sites fall on the resulting interface.

CD74 ↔ GPR75 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.42 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel in every database checked (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0) and topologically plausible as a secreted or surface CD74 ectodomain meeting the plasma-membrane GPCR GPR75. However, UniProt annotates both as glycoproteins (CD74 P04233: 6 glycosylation sites plus Proteoglycan keyword; GPR75 O95800: 3 glycosylation sites), which AF3's bare-sequence model does not represent, and whether these sites sit on the modeled interface could not be checked because no fold was available in this run. The supplied ipTM of 0.42 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, so the model itself is not positive evidence despite the interface PAE of 3.56 A and the partner being confident at only 1 of 11 receptors tested.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt CD74 (P04233) carries the keywords Glycoprotein, Proteoglycan and Phosphoprotein, with 6 annotated glycosylation features, 3 disulfide bonds and 1 modified residue; nearly all of CD74 beyond its short cytoplasmic tail and single transmembrane helix is luminal/extracellular, which is where the glycans lie. UniProt GPR75 (O95800) carries the keyword Glycoprotein with 3 annotated glycosylation features. The PubMed PTM searches returned 135 hits for CD74 and 11 for GPR75, but the top titles are about MIF-CD74 signalling, transcriptomics and 20-HETE/GPR75 pharmacology rather than glycosylation, so they add nothing beyond the UniProt annotation. Whether any glycosylation site falls on the modeled interface residues was not determined, because no fold with per-residue interface analysis was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CD74 and GPR75 returned a count of 0 with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CD74 and GPR75. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction for the pair. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. UniProt names CCL5/RANTES as the activating ligand of GPR75 and MIF as the cytokine ligand of CD74, so neither protein's documented partners include the other.
Other subunits
HLA-DRA;HLA-DRB1;MIF
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold CD74 with GPR75 and inspect the structural analysis, to establish (1) which topological side of each protein the interface uses, and in particular whether it touches both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues at once, and (2) whether any of the 6 CD74 or 3 GPR75 UniProt glycosylation sites sits on the interface. If one does, re-fold with the core N-glycan attached at that site and see whether the interface survives. CD74 is a native homotrimer that assembles with MHC class II alpha/beta heterodimers (the other_subunits listed come from the UniProt function text, since CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein), so a fold of the CD74 trimer, or CD74 with HLA-DRA/HLA-DRB1, against GPR75 would test whether the modeled surface is free in the physiological assembly. Because ipTM is 0.42, do not commit bench time before a re-fold reaches chain-pair ipTM of at least 0.5; if it does, test by co-immunoprecipitation or a surface binding assay in a cell type expressing both, for example microglia.

CFC1 ↔ GPR32 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.45 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check flagged a topology violation. The modeled CFC1–GPR32 interface lies on GPR32's cytoplasmic face. CFC1 is a GPI-anchored or secreted extracellular protein, so it has no cytosolic surface and cannot reach that face. This undercuts the low-PAE contact, because the contact is geometrically impossible in a cell. The ipTM of 0.45 was already below 0.5. There is also no STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed support, and the other evidence is weak: HPA gives no localisation and no shared enriched tissue. The HPA "plausible" flag only shows that nothing excludes co-expression. It does not fix the topology problem. GPR32's known ligands are lipid mediators, not proteins. The glycosylation caveat is secondary. I'm changing from INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE to IMPLAUSIBLE, with medium confidence because only one fold was run and it was not tested against the extracellular face.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates CFC1 as a glycoprotein and lipoprotein with a GPI anchor (1 glycosylation site, 1 lipidation site, 3 disulfide bonds). GPR32 is annotated as a glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond; GPCR N-glycosylation sites are typically on the extracellular N-terminus or loops, which is the only surface a secreted or GPI-anchored CFC1 could contact. The PubMed PTM searches (4 hits for CFC1, 7 for GPR32) returned no titles that specifically address glycosylation of either protein. Whether any glycan sits on the modelled interface is unknown, because no per-residue interface data were available and no fold could be run. Glycan occlusion of the modelled contact is a real but unverified caveat.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CFC1 and GPR32 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength=none). The pair is novel but has no supporting literature.
Other subunits
ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C;NODAL
Follow-up (not yet executed): If the pair is still of interest, re-fold with the GPR32 extracellular N-terminus and loops, and check whether any extracellular-face contact scores confidently.

IL36RN ↔ GPR87 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.67 · pTM
Rationale
IL36RN is a secreted cytokine and GPR87 is a 7-transmembrane cell-membrane GPCR, so the pairing is topologically plausible, and it is unreported (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions). However, UniProt annotates GPR87 (Q9BY21) as a Glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites, which on a GPCR sit on the small extracellular face, the only surface a secreted cytokine can reach. The AF3 interface is large (2618 Å2, 526 contacts) and was modelled without glycans, so it may be sterically blocked in vivo; whether any of the 3 sites actually falls on the interface was not checked, because no fold could be run in this triage. Two further cautions: GPR87's documented ligand is a lipid (lysophosphatidic acid), and IL36RN's documented receptor is IL1RL2, an unrelated receptor class.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists GPR87 (Q9BY21) with the Glycoprotein keyword, 3 Glycosylation features and 1 disulfide bond. Glycosylation sites on a GPCR lie on the extracellular N-terminus or loops, which is the surface a secreted cytokine would have to bind, so the concern is plausibly relevant to this interface; the exact site positions relative to the modelled contact residues were not determined. UniProt lists IL36RN (Q9UBH0) with 1 disulfide bond and no glycosylation or lipidation, so there is no concern on that side. The PubMed PTM searches returned 2 hits for IL36RN and 7 for GPR87, but the titles returned concern unrelated topics (tumour signalling, fasting, melanoma bioinformatics) and add no site-specific PTM information.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for IL36RN and GPR87 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for IL36RN and GPR87. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR87 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is novel as far as these three sources show.
Other subunits
IL1RL2;IL1RAP;SIGIRR
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1. Re-fold IL36RN with GPR87 and read off the interface residues against the 3 UniProt N-glycosylation sites of GPR87 (Q9BY21) and against the membrane topology, confirming the contacts are on the extracellular N-terminus, loops or transmembrane bundle and not on cytoplasmic residues. 2. If a glycosylation site lies on the interface, re-fold with the core glycan attached at that site and see whether the interface survives. 3. If no site lies on the interface, this becomes a novel candidate: the supplied specificity data are encouraging (confident against 1 of 5 receptors tested, p_twoway = 0.045), but ipTM 0.67 and interface pLDDT 70.2 are moderate. 4. As a positive control, fold IL36RN with its documented receptor IL1RL2 and compare the IL36RN surface used; if the GPR87 interface uses the same face, the two would compete. The other_subunits listed (IL1RL2, IL1RAP, SIGIRR) are IL36RN's partners named in its UniProt function text, not known GPR87 complex members. 5. Wet-lab test: binding of recombinant IL36RN to GPR87-expressing cells, with and without PNGase F treatment, in keratinocytes or esophageal squamous cells.

HCST ↔ GPR65 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.62 · pTM
Rationale
The real AF3 fold shows the HCST–GPR65 interface touching both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. That is impossible across an intact membrane for a single-pass adapter like HCST packing against a 7-TM receptor. My earlier LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX call ignored this. The large interface (about 2994 Å2 buried, 574 contacts) fits HCST being draped across the receptor, not a real transmembrane contact. The moderate ipTM (0.62) and the specificity data (1 of 11 partner receptors confident) do not rescue a pose that breaks membrane topology. Independent support is also missing: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all show nothing, so known_interaction_strength is none, and HPA plausibility is unknown. The KLRK1 and HCST-homodimer subunit argument was only a hypothesis and cannot justify a topologically invalid pose. GPR65 glycosylation is a side issue, since the pose itself is already invalid.
PTM / glycosylation
GPR65 has 3 annotated glycosylation sites, which on a GPCR are expected on the extracellular N-terminus or loops. It is unknown whether any lie on the interface. This is secondary to the topology violation.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for HCST and GPR65 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association. CellPhoneDB: no curated pair, and GPR65 is not in its gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0. known_interaction_strength=none, so there is no evidence of a known interaction.
Other subunits
KLRK1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Only if there is independent biological reason to suspect an HCST–GPR65 association: re-fold with KLRK1 and the HCST homodimer, and check that any interface is confined to the transmembrane segment. Otherwise treat the pair as an artifact.

CATSPER2 ↔ GPR25 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.63 · pTM
Rationale
CATSPER2 (UniProt Q96P56) is a pore-forming subunit of the sperm-specific CatSper calcium channel in the sperm flagellum, whereas GPR25 (UniProt O00155) is the CXCL17 chemokine receptor acting on lymphocytes. HPA shows no shared tissue (CATSPER2 enhanced in retina at 21.4 nTPM and testis at 14.9 nTPM; GPR25 enriched only in stomach at 1.8 nTPM), and both are multi-pass membrane proteins that would have to sit in the same membrane of the same cell to interact. The AF3 interface (ipTM 0.63, interface PAE 3.59 A, interface pLDDT 75.3, confident against 1 of 10 receptors tested, p_twoway 0.0228) is most likely transmembrane-helix packing between two bare helical bundles modelled without a bilayer and without CATSPER2's obligate channel partners. No database or literature support exists for the pair.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists no glycosylation or lipidation features for either protein; GPR25 has 1 disulfide bond, which is not a concern for the model. The PubMed PTM search returned 2 hits for CATSPER2, neither about a PTM (a beta-defensin 20 docking study and the 2003 CatSper2 hyperactivated-motility paper), and 0 hits for GPR25. There is no evidence for a PTM or glycan artifact; the concern is cell-type mismatch and missing channel subunits instead.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CATSPER2 and GPR25 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for the pair. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR25 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported, but the absence of prior evidence here reflects biological implausibility rather than a promising novel finding.
Other subunits
CATSPERB;CATSPERD;CATSPER1;CATSPER3;CATSPER4
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold was run for this candidate. If it is pursued despite the expression mismatch, first check single-cell data for any GPR25 expression in spermatids or sperm. Then re-fold CATSPER2 with GPR25 in the context of the CatSper pore tetramer (CATSPER1, CATSPER3 and CATSPER4; CellPhoneDB also lists CATSPERB and CATSPERD as complex partners; the pore subunits are from the UniProt function text and general knowledge, not a tool hit) to see whether the GPR25 contact surface is the one normally buried by neighbouring pore subunits. Also inspect interface topology for contacts that mix cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which would indicate a membrane-topology artifact.

FGA ↔ GPR39 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows that the modeled FGA–GPR39 interface includes both cytoplasmic and extracellular GPR39 residues. A secreted protein cannot contact both sides of an intact membrane, so the pose is not physiological. My original LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX verdict treated the fold as a weak but usable hint (ipTM 0.57, interface pLDDT 55.7), and that no longer holds. The same check puts a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the interface. Both proteins are UniProt glycoproteins (FGA has 4 glycosylation sites, GPR39 has 3), and AF3 does not model glycans. Any real contact would therefore probably be shaped by glycans that the bare-sequence model leaves out. The earlier points stand: HPA compartments are plausible (secreted vs. membrane), STRING and CellPhoneDB report no association, and the only PubMed hit is an unrelated methylation-signature co-listing. FGA also belongs to the obligate fibrinogen complex (FGA/FGB/FGG), which the single-chain fold ignores. I am not moving to IMPLAUSIBLE because the compartments could still meet extracellularly. But the structural evidence is now an artifact and not support, so nothing credible is left for a specific FGA–GPR39 interaction.
PTM / glycosylation
Both FGA (P02671) and GPR39 (O43194) are annotated glycoproteins. The fold check places a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled A-B interface, and AF3 does not represent glycans. This makes the modeled contact unreliable.
PubMed co-occurrence
One PubMed hit (PMID 37839873), in which both genes appear in a prognostic methylation signature for lung squamous cell carcinoma. It is unrelated to any physical interaction between the two proteins.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING and CellPhoneDB both report nothing for this pair. The only PubMed hit (PMID 37839873) is a gene-signature co-listing, not a binding report. The pair is unreported, so it is not ALREADY_KNOWN.
Other subunits
FGB;FGG
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-model GPR39 against the full fibrinogen complex (FGA, FGB, FGG), restricting contacts to the extracellular N-terminus and loops. Represent the glycans if possible. Treat any pose that touches GPR39 cytoplasmic residues as invalid.

TDGF1 ↔ GPR31 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.58 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows the modeled TDGF1–GPR31 interface includes both cytoplasmic and extracellular GPR31 residues. That is topologically impossible across an intact membrane, because TDGF1 is an extracellular, GPI-anchored or shed protein and cannot reach the cytoplasmic side. This undercuts my earlier reliance on the moderate ipTM of 0.58 and the partner-specific gate result, since the pose is physically unachievable. The other evidence is neutral. HPA co-expression is plausible, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed show no prior link (known_interaction_strength=none), but none of that rescues an impossible binding mode. Part of the interface could still be a genuine extracellular contact, but the supplied model does not show one. I therefore downgrade from CONFIRMED_NOVEL to IMPLAUSIBLE for this modeled pose.
PTM / glycosylation
Both proteins are glycoproteins with one site each, and TDGF1 has a GPI anchor not modeled by AF3. Neither is a lectin, so this is a minor caveat.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for TDGF1 and GPR31 returned 0 papers.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING no association, CellPhoneDB none (GPR31 not in its gene table), PubMed 0 co-mentions: no known interaction.
Other subunits
NODAL;ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold TDGF1 against only the extracellular domain of GPR31 (N-terminus and extracellular loops), with the transmembrane and cytoplasmic regions masked. Alternatively, test a TDGF1 complex with a known co-receptor. Keep the pair only if an interface confined to the extracellular side still scores well.

Rationale

No STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed link exists, and HPA gave no expression or localization data that could rescue the pair, so nothing offsets the topology problem. I rate confidence medium because the lack of expression data means co-localization is unknown rather than disproven.

Rationale

UNC5D ↔ GPR179 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.44 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows two problems that my first INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE verdict did not address. First, the A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues of two single-membrane proteins, which cannot happen across an intact membrane. That is consistent with the weak scores (ipTM 0.44, interface pLDDT 48.2) and the clashing, entangled geometry (min separation 1.89 Å, about 20,700 Å² buried), so the model is a low-confidence artifact and not a binding mode. Second, a glycosylation or lipidation site lies directly on the modeled interface. AF3 does not represent glycans, so any apparent contact there is unreliable. Nothing outside the fold supports the pair: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 papers) all return nothing, and HPA plausibility is unknown. No substantiated co-localization or expression evidence offsets the invalid topology. I am therefore moving to IMPLAUSIBLE, with medium confidence because both proteins are neuronal membrane proteins and a real ectodomain-ectodomain interaction is not formally ruled out.
PTM / glycosylation
The fold analysis puts a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled interface. Both proteins are glycoproteins (UNC5D has 4 sites, GPR179 has 2). AF3 omits glycans, so the interface is not reliable as modeled.
PubMed co-occurrence

COL26A1 ↔ GPR149 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.24 · pTM
Rationale
The fold check shows two problems that my first verdict did not address. (1) Topology violation: the modeled COL26A1–GPR149 interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues of GPR149. A secreted collagen cannot reach the cytoplasmic face of an intact plasma membrane, so the predicted contact cannot be physical. This is consistent with the weak scores (ipTM 0.24, interface pLDDT 44.5, p=0.054). (2) A glycosylation or lipidation site sits directly on the modeled interface. UniProt lists glycosylation sites on both proteins and AF3 does not model glycans. The contact is therefore a bare-sequence artifact at a site that is normally modified, and I previously treated this only as an unchecked caveat. Neither the PTM issue nor the topology issue is offset by positive evidence. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all returned nothing. The only support is that both genes are brain-enhanced and the compartments are compatible in principle (HPA plausibility "plausible"). That is not enough to rescue a structurally impossible interface. I am changing the verdict from INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE to IMPLAUSIBLE for this modeled interaction. A true extracellular-domain interaction is not strictly excluded, but this fold gives no support for it.
PTM / glycosylation
COL26A1 has 2 glycosylation sites and GPR149 has 3. The fold flags a glycosylation or lipidation site directly on the modeled interface. AF3 does not represent glycans, so the modeled contact is unreliable at that site.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for COL26A1 and GPR149 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence (0 papers) all returned nothing for this pair (known_interaction_strength=none). GPR149 is an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
COL26A1 (homotrimer; collagen chains assemble as trimers, so the single-chain model may not represent the native state)
Follow-up (not yet executed): If pursued, re-model only the GPR149 extracellular N-terminal region with COL26A1, using glycans where possible. Otherwise validate experimentally, for example by co-IP or a binding assay.

GRIN2A ↔ GPR12 IMPLAUSIBLE medium confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
I'm changing the verdict from LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX to IMPLAUSIBLE, for these reasons. (1) Lectin flag: I don't think it applies to this pair. GPR12 is an orphan, constitutively Gs-coupled class A GPCR. It is not a galectin, siglec or other glycan-binding lectin. Its UniProt record lists glycosylation sites, but that is a PTM, not a carbohydrate-binding mechanism. The flag looks like a false positive from text matching, so I'm not using it as grounds for LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM. I could not re-read the UniProt FUNCTION text in this session, so this rests on what I know about GPR12. Even so, AF3 omits glycans and lipids entirely, which weakens trust in the modelled contact. (2) Topology violation: this is decisive. The modelled A-B interface spans both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which an intact membrane makes impossible. The fold's other weaknesses fit that picture: the interface is huge (4655 Å², 785 contacts), the minimum separation of 1.73 Å is closer than a normal non-covalent contact, ipTM is only 0.57, and interface pLDDT is 61.5. A glycosylation/lipidation site also sits directly on the interface, and AF3 cannot model either modification. The prediction is therefore more likely a bare-sequence packing artifact than a real lateral contact. My earlier subcomplex reasoning (GRIN1 missing from the model) was an excuse for a poor fold, not support for the interaction. No independent evidence supports the pair: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-mention all returned nothing. Co-expression in brain makes contact physically possible but is not evidence that it happens.
PTM / glycosylation
GRIN2A has 7 glycosylation sites. GPR12 has 2 glycosylation sites and 1 palmitoylation site. The fold flagged a glycosylation/lipidation site directly on the modelled interface, and AF3 cannot represent these modifications. GPR12 is not a lectin, so this is a fold-reliability concern and not a glycan-mediated binding mechanism.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for GRIN2A and GPR12 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (GPR12 absent from the gene table). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. No known-interaction support.
Other subunits
GRIN1
Follow-up (not yet executed): No further follow-up is warranted unless independent evidence appears, such as co-IP, proximity labelling or a brain co-expression dataset. If pursued, re-model with GRIN1 and a membrane-topology-aware setup.
